    60	/// Comparison operator parsed from a criteria string like `">5"`.
    61	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
    62	enum CmpOp {
    63	    Eq,
    64	    Ne,
    65	    Lt,
    66	    Le,
    67	    Gt,
    68	    Ge,
    69	}
    70	
    71	/// Compiled predicate that decides whether a cell value matches
    72	/// the user-supplied criteria.
    73	enum Predicate {
    74	    /// Compare a numeric cell value against a numeric target.
    75	    /// Non-numeric cell values never match.
    76	    Numeric(CmpOp, f64),
    77	    /// Compare a text cell value against a text target (case-
    78	    /// insensitive). For Eq/Ne, numeric-text equivalence is also
    79	    /// honored: `=5` matches Value::Number(5.0) AND Value::Text("5").
    80	    /// For ordered comparisons (Lt/Le/Gt/Ge), Text-only ordering.
    81	    Text(CmpOp, String),
    82	    /// Boolean equality / inequality.
    83	    Boolean(CmpOp, bool),
    84	    /// Blank-criteria match: criteria is `""` (empty text) → matches
    85	    /// Value::Blank AND Value::Text("").
    86	    Blank(CmpOp),
    87	}
    88	
    89	impl Predicate {
    90	    fn matches(&self, v: &Value) -> bool {
    91	        match self {
    92	            Predicate::Numeric(op, target) => match v {
    93	                Value::Number(n) => apply_cmp(*op, *n, *target),
    94	                Value::Boolean(b) => apply_cmp(*op, if *b { 1.0 } else { 0.0 }, *target),
    95	                Value::Text(s) => {
    96	                    // Lenient: if the text parses as a number, compare
    97	                    // numerically. Otherwise Eq always false, Ne always
    98	                    // true (mirrors Excel "text never equals number").
    99	                    if let Ok(n) = s.as_ref().trim().parse::<f64>() {
   100	                        apply_cmp(*op, n, *target)
   101	                    } else {
   102	                        matches!(op, CmpOp::Ne)
   103	                    }
   104	                }
   105	                Value::Blank => match op {
   106	                    // Numeric Eq against blank: blank coerces to 0 in
   107	                    // numeric context per Excel SUMIF canon.
   108	                    CmpOp::Eq => *target == 0.0,
   109	                    CmpOp::Ne => *target != 0.0,
   110	                    _ => apply_cmp(*op, 0.0, *target),
   111	                },
   112	                Value::Error(_) => false,
   113	            },
   114	            Predicate::Text(op, target) => match v {
   115	                Value::Text(s) => apply_text_cmp(*op, s.as_ref(), target),
   116	                Value::Number(n) => {
   117	                    // Numeric vs text criteria: Eq/Ne are honored if
   118	                    // the target text parses as the same number.
   119	                    if let Ok(t) = target.parse::<f64>() {
   120	                        apply_cmp(*op, *n, t)
   121	                    } else {
   122	                        matches!(op, CmpOp::Ne)
   123	                    }
   124	                }
   125	                Value::Boolean(b) => {
   126	                    let bs = if *b { "TRUE" } else { "FALSE" };
   127	                    apply_text_cmp(*op, bs, target)
   128	                }
   129	                Value::Blank => apply_text_cmp(*op, "", target),
   130	                Value::Error(_) => false,
   131	            },
   132	            Predicate::Boolean(op, target) => match v {
   133	                Value::Boolean(b) => match op {
   134	                    CmpOp::Eq => *b == *target,
   135	                    CmpOp::Ne => *b != *target,
   136	                    _ => false, // No ordering on bools.
   137	                },
   138	                _ => matches!(op, CmpOp::Ne),
   139	            },
   140	            Predicate::Blank(op) => {
   141	                let is_blank = matches!(v, Value::Blank)
   142	                    || matches!(v, Value::Text(s) if s.as_ref().is_empty());
   143	                match op {
   144	                    CmpOp::Eq => is_blank,
   145	                    CmpOp::Ne => !is_blank,
   146	                    _ => false,
   147	                }
   148	            }
   149	        }
   150	    }
   151	}
   152	
   153	fn apply_cmp(op: CmpOp, lhs: f64, rhs: f64) -> bool {
   154	    match op {
   155	        CmpOp::Eq => lhs == rhs,
   156	        CmpOp::Ne => lhs != rhs,
   157	        CmpOp::Lt => lhs < rhs,
   158	        CmpOp::Le => lhs <= rhs,
   159	        CmpOp::Gt => lhs > rhs,
   160	        CmpOp::Ge => lhs >= rhs,
   161	    }
   162	}
   163	
   164	fn apply_text_cmp(op: CmpOp, lhs: &str, rhs: &str) -> bool {
   165	    let l = lhs.to_uppercase();
   166	    let r = rhs.to_uppercase();
   167	    match op {
   168	        CmpOp::Eq => l == r,
   169	        CmpOp::Ne => l != r,
   170	        CmpOp::Lt => l < r,
   171	        CmpOp::Le => l <= r,
   172	        CmpOp::Gt => l > r,
   173	        CmpOp::Ge => l >= r,
   174	    }
   175	}
   176	
   177	/// Parse a user-supplied criteria `Value` into a `Predicate`. Returns
   178	/// `Err(#VALUE!)` if the criteria can't be interpreted.
   179	fn build_predicate(criteria: &Value) -> Result<Predicate, ErrorValue> {
   180	    match criteria {
   181	        Value::Error(e) => Err(*e),
   182	        Value::Number(n) => Ok(Predicate::Numeric(CmpOp::Eq, *n)),
   183	        Value::Boolean(b) => Ok(Predicate::Boolean(CmpOp::Eq, *b)),
   184	        Value::Blank => Ok(Predicate::Blank(CmpOp::Eq)),
   185	        Value::Text(s) => {
   186	            let s = s.as_ref();
   187	            // Operator prefix: ">=" "<=" "<>" ">" "<" "=" (in length
   188	            // order so two-char ops are tried first).
   189	            let (op, rest) = if let Some(r) = s.strip_prefix(">=") {
   190	                (CmpOp::Ge, r)
   191	            } else if let Some(r) = s.strip_prefix("<=") {
   192	                (CmpOp::Le, r)
   193	            } else if let Some(r) = s.strip_prefix("<>") {
   194	                (CmpOp::Ne, r)
   195	            } else if let Some(r) = s.strip_prefix('>') {
   196	                (CmpOp::Gt, r)
   197	            } else if let Some(r) = s.strip_prefix('<') {
   198	                (CmpOp::Lt, r)
   199	            } else if let Some(r) = s.strip_prefix('=') {
   200	                (CmpOp::Eq, r)
   201	            } else {
   202	                (CmpOp::Eq, s)
   203	            };
   204	            let rest = rest.trim();
   205	            // Empty rest after stripping `<>` / `=` etc. is the blank
   206	            // match.
   207	            if rest.is_empty() {
   208	                return Ok(Predicate::Blank(op));
   209	            }
   210	            // Try to parse as a number first; fall back to text/bool.
   211	            if let Ok(n) = rest.parse::<f64>() {
   212	                return Ok(Predicate::Numeric(op, n));
   213	            }
   214	            // Bool: case-insensitive TRUE / FALSE.
   215	            let upper = rest.to_ascii_uppercase();
   216	            if upper == "TRUE" {
   217	                return Ok(Predicate::Boolean(op, true));
   218	            }
   219	            if upper == "FALSE" {
   220	                return Ok(Predicate::Boolean(op, false));
   221	            }
   222	            // Default: text comparison.
   223	            Ok(Predicate::Text(op, rest.to_string()))
   224	        }
   225	    }
   226	}
   227	
   228	/// `SUMIF(range, criteria, [sum_range])` — sum cells in `sum_range`
   229	/// (or `range` if `sum_range` omitted) where the corresponding cell
   230	/// in `range` matches `criteria`.
   231	///
   232	/// Shape: the two ranges are paired element-wise. If `sum_range` is
   233	/// shorter than `range`, missing cells are treated as if absent (no
   234	/// contribution). If `sum_range` is longer, extras are ignored.
   235	/// This matches Excel's "anchor the top-left, ignore mismatched
   236	/// shape beyond the criteria range's footprint" behavior in the
   237	/// common case.
   238	pub fn sumif(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
   239	    if args.len() < 2 || args.len() > 3 {
   240	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   241	    }
   242	    let crit_range = match &args[0] {
   243	        FnArg::Range { values, .. } => values.as_slice(),
   244	        FnArg::Scalar(_) => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   245	    };
   246	    let crit = match &args[1] {
   247	        FnArg::Scalar(v) => v,
   248	        FnArg::Range { .. } => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   249	    };
   250	    let sum_range_owned;
   251	    let sum_range: &[Value] = if args.len() == 3 {
   252	        match &args[2] {
   253	            FnArg::Range { values, .. } => values.as_slice(),
   254	            FnArg::Scalar(_) => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   255	        }
   256	    } else {
   257	        // Default: sum the criteria range itself.
   258	        sum_range_owned = crit_range;
   259	        sum_range_owned
   260	    };
   261	
   262	    let predicate = match build_predicate(crit) {
   263	        Ok(p) => p,
   264	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   265	    };
   266	
   267	    let mut total = 0.0_f64;
   268	    for (c, s) in crit_range.iter().zip(sum_range.iter()) {
   269	        if !predicate.matches(c) {
   270	            continue;
   271	        }
   272	        match coerce_numeric(s) {
   273	            NumericArg::Number(n) => total += n,
   274	            NumericArg::Skip => {}
   275	            // Excel: if a sum_range cell holds an error, the error
   276	            // propagates. Match that.
   277	            NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   278	        }
   279	    }
   280	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(total) {
   281	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   282	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   283	    }
   284	}
   285	
   286	// ===== Lookup family (W5-54, Phase 4.3 V2 batch #3) =====
   287	//
   288	// VLOOKUP / HLOOKUP / MATCH / INDEX / CHOOSE. All consume the
   289	// shape-aware `FnArg::Range { values, rows, cols }` introduced in
   290	// W5-54 (or `FnArg::Scalar` for CHOOSE). Row/col addressing is
   291	// 0-based internally; Excel-facing args are 1-based.
   292	
   293	/// Compare two `Value`s for equality per Excel's MATCH/VLOOKUP rule:
   294	/// text comparisons are case-insensitive; numbers and bools compare
   295	/// strictly; numeric-text DOES match the corresponding number (e.g.
   296	/// `Text("5")` matches `Number(5.0)` for lookup_value=5); Blank
   297	/// matches Blank only; Error never matches.
   298	fn lookup_eq(needle: &Value, hay: &Value) -> bool {
   299	    match (needle, hay) {
   300	        (Value::Error(_), _) | (_, Value::Error(_)) => false,
   301	        (Value::Blank, Value::Blank) => true,
   302	        (Value::Blank, _) | (_, Value::Blank) => false,
   303	        (Value::Boolean(a), Value::Boolean(b)) => a == b,
   304	        (Value::Number(a), Value::Number(b)) => a == b,
   305	        (Value::Text(a), Value::Text(b)) => a.eq_ignore_ascii_case(b),
   306	        (Value::Number(a), Value::Text(t)) | (Value::Text(t), Value::Number(a)) => {
   307	            t.parse::<f64>().map(|n| n == *a).unwrap_or(false)
   308	        }
   309	        // Bool vs other: no implicit coercion for lookup equality.
   310	        (Value::Boolean(_), _) | (_, Value::Boolean(_)) => false,
   311	    }
   312	}
   313	
   314	/// Compare two `Value`s for ordered comparison (used by approximate
   315	/// MATCH/VLOOKUP). Returns `Some(ordering)` if comparable, `None`
   316	/// otherwise (errors, type mismatch). Numbers compare numerically;
   317	/// text compares case-insensitively as strings; bools compare false
   318	/// < true. Cross-type comparison returns None.
   319	fn lookup_cmp(a: &Value, b: &Value) -> Option<std::cmp::Ordering> {
   320	    use std::cmp::Ordering;
   321	    match (a, b) {
   322	        (Value::Error(_), _) | (_, Value::Error(_)) => None,
   323	        (Value::Number(x), Value::Number(y)) => x.partial_cmp(y),
   324	        (Value::Text(x), Value::Text(y)) => {
   325	            let xu = x.to_uppercase();
   326	            let yu = y.to_uppercase();
   327	            Some(xu.cmp(&yu))
   328	        }
   329	        (Value::Boolean(x), Value::Boolean(y)) => Some(x.cmp(y)),
   330	        (Value::Blank, Value::Blank) => Some(Ordering::Equal),
   331	        // Treat Blank as 0 for numeric comparison (Excel canon).
   332	        (Value::Blank, Value::Number(y)) => 0.0_f64.partial_cmp(y),
   333	        (Value::Number(x), Value::Blank) => x.partial_cmp(&0.0_f64),
   334	        _ => None,
   335	    }
   336	}
   337	
   338	/// `MATCH(lookup_value, lookup_array, [match_type])` — return the
   339	/// 1-based position of `lookup_value` in `lookup_array`. Match types:
   340	/// - **0** (exact): linear scan; first equal match wins. Most common
   341	///   form; the only safe choice for unsorted data.
   342	/// - **1** (largest ≤, default): linear scan; return the position of
   343	///   the largest value ≤ lookup_value. **Array must be sorted
   344	///   ascending; un-sorted input yields undefined results per Excel
   345	///   canon.** V1 implementation is linear (not binary search) for
   346	///   simplicity; revisit if perf shows.
   347	/// - **-1** (smallest ≥): linear scan; smallest value ≥ lookup_value.
   348	///   Array must be sorted descending.
   349	///
   350	/// Returns `#N/A` if not found. The `lookup_array` should be 1D
   351	/// (single row or single column); 2D is treated as flat row-major.
   352	pub fn r#match(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
   353	    if args.len() < 2 || args.len() > 3 {
   354	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   355	    }
   356	    let needle = match &args[0] {
   357	        FnArg::Scalar(v) => v,
   358	        FnArg::Range { .. } => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   359	    };
   360	    if let Value::Error(e) = needle {
   361	        return Value::Error(*e);
   362	    }
   363	    let hay = match &args[1] {
   364	        FnArg::Range { values, .. } => values.as_slice(),
   365	        FnArg::Scalar(_) => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   366	    };
   367	    let match_type: i32 = if args.len() == 3 {
   368	        match &args[2] {
   369	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Number(n)) => n.trunc() as i32,
   370	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Blank) => 1,
   371	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Boolean(b)) => {
   372	                if *b {
   373	                    1
   374	                } else {
   375	                    0
   376	                }
   377	            }
   378	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Error(e)) => return Value::Error(*e),
   379	            _ => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   380	        }
   381	    } else {
   382	        1
   383	    };
   384	
   385	    use std::cmp::Ordering;
   386	    match match_type {
   387	        0 => {
   388	            for (i, cell) in hay.iter().enumerate() {
   389	                if lookup_eq(needle, cell) {
   390	                    return Value::Number((i + 1) as f64);
   391	                }
   392	            }
   393	            Value::Error(ErrorValue::NA)
   394	        }
   395	        1 => {
   396	            // Largest value ≤ needle. Assumes ascending sort; we
   397	            // walk while cell ≤ needle and remember the last hit.
   398	            let mut found: Option<usize> = None;
   399	            for (i, cell) in hay.iter().enumerate() {
   400	                match lookup_cmp(cell, needle) {
   401	                    Some(Ordering::Less) | Some(Ordering::Equal) => found = Some(i),
   402	                    Some(Ordering::Greater) => break,
   403	                    None => continue,
   404	                }
   405	            }
   406	            match found {
   407	                Some(i) => Value::Number((i + 1) as f64),
   408	                None => Value::Error(ErrorValue::NA),
   409	            }
   410	        }
   411	        -1 => {
   412	            // Smallest value ≥ needle. Assumes descending sort.
   413	            let mut found: Option<usize> = None;
   414	            for (i, cell) in hay.iter().enumerate() {
   415	                match lookup_cmp(cell, needle) {
   416	                    Some(Ordering::Greater) | Some(Ordering::Equal) => found = Some(i),
   417	                    Some(Ordering::Less) => break,
   418	                    None => continue,
   419	                }
   420	            }

   421	            match found {
   422	                Some(i) => Value::Number((i + 1) as f64),
   423	                None => Value::Error(ErrorValue::NA),
   424	            }
   425	        }
   426	        _ => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   427	    }
   428	}

   430	/// `INDEX(array, row_num, [col_num])` — return the element at the
   431	/// (1-based) row + col position of `array`. For a 1D array (single
   432	/// row or column), `col_num` is optional and the second arg is
   433	/// interpreted as the index along the array's axis.
   434	///
   435	/// V1 returns scalar results only. `row_num = 0` (return whole
   436	/// column) and `col_num = 0` (return whole row) are array-return
   437	/// modes that need spill semantics — Phase 4.7 ARR work; for now
   438	/// they return `#REF!` to avoid silently returning the first cell.
   439	/// Out-of-bounds → `#REF!`.
   440	pub fn index(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
   441	    if args.len() < 2 || args.len() > 3 {
   442	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   443	    }
   444	    let (values, rows, cols) = match &args[0] {
   445	        FnArg::Range { values, rows, cols } => (values.as_slice(), *rows, *cols),
   446	        FnArg::Scalar(_) => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   447	    };
   448	    if values.is_empty() {
   449	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref);
   450	    }
   451	
   452	    let scalar_to_idx = |v: &Value| -> Result<i64, ErrorValue> {
   453	        match v {
   454	            Value::Number(n) => Ok(n.trunc() as i64),
   455	            Value::Boolean(true) => Ok(1),
   456	            Value::Boolean(false) => Ok(0),
   457	            Value::Blank => Ok(0),
   458	            Value::Error(e) => Err(*e),
   459	            Value::Text(_) => Err(ErrorValue::Value),
   460	        }
   461	    };
   462	
   463	    let row_num = match &args[1] {
   464	        FnArg::Scalar(v) => match scalar_to_idx(v) {
   465	            Ok(i) => i,
   466	            Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   467	        },
   468	        FnArg::Range { .. } => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   469	    };
   470	    let col_num: i64 = if args.len() == 3 {
   471	        match &args[2] {
   472	            FnArg::Scalar(v) => match scalar_to_idx(v) {
   473	                Ok(i) => i,
   474	                Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   475	            },
   476	            FnArg::Range { .. } => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   477	        }
   478	    } else if rows == 1 {
   479	        // Single-row array + only row_num supplied: interpret
   480	        // row_num as the column index along the single row.
   481	        let idx = row_num;
   482	        return index_1d(values, idx, cols);
   483	    } else if cols == 1 {
   484	        // Single-column array + only row_num supplied: row_num IS
   485	        // the position along the column.
   486	        return index_1d(values, row_num, rows);
   487	    } else {
   488	        // 2D array but only one arg → ambiguous. Excel canon:
   489	        // `INDEX(2D, n)` returns the nth row as an array, which
   490	        // we don't support yet.
   491	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref);
   492	    };
   493	
   494	    // 0,0 → whole array (spill, not supported V1).
   495	    if row_num == 0 && col_num == 0 {
   496	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref);
   497	    }
   498	    // 0 in one dimension → whole row/col spill (not supported V1).
   499	    if row_num == 0 || col_num == 0 {
   500	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref);
   501	    }
   502	    // Negative or zero → #VALUE! per Excel.
   503	    if row_num < 1 || col_num < 1 {
   504	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   505	    }
   506	    let r = (row_num - 1) as usize;
   507	    let c = (col_num - 1) as usize;
   508	    if r >= rows || c >= cols {
   509	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref);
   510	    }
   511	    values[r * cols + c].clone()
   512	}
   513	
   514	/// Helper for INDEX 1D case: array is single row OR single column,
   515	/// `idx` is 1-based, `axis_len` is the number of elements along the
   516	/// non-degenerate axis.
   517	fn index_1d(values: &[Value], idx: i64, axis_len: usize) -> Value {
   518	    if idx == 0 {
   519	        // Whole-array spill — not supported V1.
   520	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref);
   521	    }
   522	    if idx < 1 {
   523	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   524	    }
   525	    let i = (idx - 1) as usize;
   526	    if i >= axis_len {
   527	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref);
   528	    }
   529	    values[i].clone()
   530	}
   531	
   532	/// Internal helper used by VLOOKUP and HLOOKUP: extract the
   533	/// "lookup column" (col 0 for VLOOKUP; the entire row 0 for HLOOKUP)
   534	/// from a 2D shape, run the appropriate MATCH (exact or
   535	/// approximate), and return the matched offset.
   536	///
   537	/// `axis_values` is the 1D vector along the search axis. Returns the
   538	/// 0-based position, or `Err(#N/A)` if not found.
   539	fn lookup_search(needle: &Value, axis_values: &[Value], exact: bool) -> Result<usize, ErrorValue> {
   540	    use std::cmp::Ordering;
   541	    if exact {
   542	        for (i, cell) in axis_values.iter().enumerate() {
   543	            if lookup_eq(needle, cell) {
   544	                return Ok(i);
   545	            }
   546	        }
   547	        Err(ErrorValue::NA)
   548	    } else {
   549	        let mut found: Option<usize> = None;
   550	        for (i, cell) in axis_values.iter().enumerate() {
   551	            match lookup_cmp(cell, needle) {
   552	                Some(Ordering::Less) | Some(Ordering::Equal) => found = Some(i),
   553	                Some(Ordering::Greater) => break,
   554	                None => continue,
   555	            }
   556	        }
   557	        found.ok_or(ErrorValue::NA)
   558	    }
   559	}
   560	
   561	/// `VLOOKUP(lookup_value, table_array, col_index_num, [range_lookup])`
   562	/// — search the FIRST COLUMN of `table_array` for `lookup_value`,
   563	/// return the value at `col_index_num` of the matched row (1-based).
   564	///
   565	/// `range_lookup`: TRUE (default) = approximate match (table must be
   566	/// sorted ascending by first column); FALSE = exact match. Not-found
   567	/// → `#N/A`. col_index_num out of range → `#REF!`.
   568	pub fn vlookup(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
   569	    if args.len() < 3 || args.len() > 4 {
   570	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   571	    }
   572	    let needle = match &args[0] {
   573	        FnArg::Scalar(v) => v,
   574	        FnArg::Range { .. } => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   575	    };
   576	    if let Value::Error(e) = needle {
   577	        return Value::Error(*e);
   578	    }
   579	    let (values, rows, cols) = match &args[1] {
   580	        FnArg::Range { values, rows, cols } => (values.as_slice(), *rows, *cols),
   581	        FnArg::Scalar(_) => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   582	    };
   583	    let col_index_num = match &args[2] {
   584	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Number(n)) => n.trunc() as i64,
   585	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Boolean(b)) => i64::from(*b),
   586	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Blank) => 0,
   587	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Error(e)) => return Value::Error(*e),
   588	        _ => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   589	    };
   590	    if col_index_num < 1 {
   591	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   592	    }
   593	    let col_index = (col_index_num - 1) as usize;
   594	    if col_index >= cols {
   595	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref);
   596	    }
   597	    let exact = if args.len() == 4 {
   598	        match &args[3] {
   599	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Boolean(b)) => !*b,
   600	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Number(n)) => *n == 0.0,
   601	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Blank) => false, // default approximate
   602	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Error(e)) => return Value::Error(*e),
   603	            _ => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   604	        }
   605	    } else {
   606	        false // default: approximate match
   607	    };
   608	
   609	    // Materialize the first column.
   610	    if rows == 0 || cols == 0 {
   611	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::NA);
   612	    }
   613	    let first_col: Vec<Value> = (0..rows).map(|r| values[r * cols].clone()).collect();
   614	    match lookup_search(needle, &first_col, exact) {
   615	        Ok(r) => values[r * cols + col_index].clone(),
   616	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   617	    }
   618	}
   619	
   620	/// `HLOOKUP(lookup_value, table_array, row_index_num, [range_lookup])`
   621	/// — mirror of VLOOKUP along rows: search the FIRST ROW of
   622	/// `table_array`, return the value at `row_index_num` of the matched
   623	/// column.
   624	pub fn hlookup(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
   625	    if args.len() < 3 || args.len() > 4 {
   626	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   627	    }
   628	    let needle = match &args[0] {
   629	        FnArg::Scalar(v) => v,
   630	        FnArg::Range { .. } => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   631	    };
   632	    if let Value::Error(e) = needle {
   633	        return Value::Error(*e);
   634	    }
   635	    let (values, rows, cols) = match &args[1] {
   636	        FnArg::Range { values, rows, cols } => (values.as_slice(), *rows, *cols),
   637	        FnArg::Scalar(_) => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   638	    };
   639	    let row_index_num = match &args[2] {
   640	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Number(n)) => n.trunc() as i64,
   641	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Boolean(b)) => i64::from(*b),
   642	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Blank) => 0,
   643	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Error(e)) => return Value::Error(*e),
   644	        _ => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   645	    };
   646	    if row_index_num < 1 {
   647	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   648	    }
   649	    let row_index = (row_index_num - 1) as usize;
   650	    if row_index >= rows {
   651	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref);
   652	    }
   653	    let exact = if args.len() == 4 {
   654	        match &args[3] {
   655	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Boolean(b)) => !*b,
   656	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Number(n)) => *n == 0.0,
   657	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Blank) => false,
   658	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Error(e)) => return Value::Error(*e),
   659	            _ => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   660	        }
   661	    } else {
   662	        false
   663	    };
   664	    if rows == 0 || cols == 0 {
   665	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::NA);
   666	    }
   667	    let first_row: Vec<Value> = values.iter().take(cols).cloned().collect();
   668	    match lookup_search(needle, &first_row, exact) {
   669	        Ok(c) => values[row_index * cols + c].clone(),
   670	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   671	    }
   672	}
   673	
   674	/// `CHOOSE(index_num, value1, [value2, ...])` — return the `index_num`-th
   675	/// value from the list (1-based). All args are scalars (no ranges).
   676	/// Excel canon: index_num must be ≥1 and ≤ count of values.
   677	pub fn choose(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
   678	    if args.len() < 2 {
   679	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   680	    }
   681	    let index_num = match &args[0] {
   682	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Number(n)) => n.trunc() as i64,
   683	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Boolean(b)) => i64::from(*b),
   684	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Blank) => 0,
   685	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Error(e)) => return Value::Error(*e),
   686	        _ => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   687	    };
   688	    if index_num < 1 {
   689	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   690	    }
   691	    let i = index_num as usize;
   692	    if i >= args.len() {
   693	        // args[0] is index, args[1..] are values; valid i is 1..args.len()-1+1 = args.len()
   694	        // But i (1-based) maps to args[i]. So valid: 1 ≤ i ≤ args.len()-1.
   695	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   696	    }
   697	    match &args[i] {
   698	        FnArg::Scalar(v) => v.clone(),
   699	        FnArg::Range { .. } => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   700	    }
   701	}
   702	
   703	/// `COUNTIF(range, criteria)` — count cells in `range` matching
   704	/// `criteria`. Returns count as a `Number`. Error in criteria → that
   705	/// error; error in a range cell → not counted (per Excel canon,
   706	/// COUNTIF ignores Error cells, unlike SUMIF which propagates).
   707	pub fn countif(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
   708	    if args.len() != 2 {
   709	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   710	    }
   711	    let range = match &args[0] {
   712	        FnArg::Range { values, .. } => values.as_slice(),
   713	        FnArg::Scalar(_) => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   714	    };
   715	    let crit = match &args[1] {
   716	        FnArg::Scalar(v) => v,
   717	        FnArg::Range { .. } => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   718	    };
   719	    let predicate = match build_predicate(crit) {
   720	        Ok(p) => p,
   721	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   722	    };
   723	    let count = range.iter().filter(|v| predicate.matches(v)).count();
   724	    Value::Number(count as f64)
   725	}
   726	
   727	// ===== W5-55: AVERAGEIF / SUMIFS / COUNTIFS / AVERAGEIFS / SUMPRODUCT =====
   728	//
   729	// Conditional-aggregate completion batch. Direct extensions of
   730	// W5-53's `build_predicate` + W5-54's range-shape infra. All ranges
   731	// in an IFS-family call must have identical row × col dimensions
   732	// (Excel canon: #VALUE! otherwise).
   733	
   734	/// `AVERAGEIF(range, criteria, [average_range])` — average cells in
   735	/// `average_range` (or `range` if omitted) where the corresponding
   736	/// cell in `range` matches `criteria`. Returns `#DIV/0!` if no cells
   737	/// match. Same predicate semantics as SUMIF.
   738	pub fn averageif(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
   739	    if args.len() < 2 || args.len() > 3 {
   740	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   741	    }
   742	    let crit_range = match &args[0] {
   743	        FnArg::Range { values, .. } => values.as_slice(),
   744	        FnArg::Scalar(_) => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   745	    };
   746	    let crit = match &args[1] {
   747	        FnArg::Scalar(v) => v,
   748	        FnArg::Range { .. } => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   749	    };
   750	    let avg_range: &[Value] = if args.len() == 3 {
   751	        match &args[2] {
   752	            FnArg::Range { values, .. } => values.as_slice(),
   753	            FnArg::Scalar(_) => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   754	        }
   755	    } else {
   756	        crit_range
   757	    };
   758	    let predicate = match build_predicate(crit) {
   759	        Ok(p) => p,
   760	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   761	    };
   762	    let mut total = 0.0_f64;
   763	    let mut count: usize = 0;
   764	    for (c, v) in crit_range.iter().zip(avg_range.iter()) {
   765	        if !predicate.matches(c) {
   766	            continue;
   767	        }
   768	        match coerce_numeric(v) {
   769	            NumericArg::Number(n) => {
   770	                total += n;
   771	                count += 1;
   772	            }
   773	            // Blank in the average range: skip (don't count). Matches
   774	            // SUMIF / AVERAGE behavior — Blank ≠ zero in averaging.
   775	            NumericArg::Skip => {}
   776	            NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   777	        }
   778	    }
   779	    if count == 0 {
   780	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero);
   781	    }
   782	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(total / (count as f64)) {
   783	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   784	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   785	    }
   786	}
   787	
   788	/// Helper: parse `[crit_range1, crit1, crit_range2, crit2, ...]`
   789	/// pairs after a starting index. Returns (paired_ranges_and_preds,
   790	/// expected_len_in_cells) or an Error to propagate.
   791	///
   792	/// For SUMIFS / AVERAGEIFS the start_index is 1 (arg[0] is the
   793	/// sum/average range). For COUNTIFS it's 0 (no leading range).
   794	///
   795	/// All ranges must have identical `.len()`. Excel canon: mismatched
   796	/// shapes → `#VALUE!`.
   797	type IfsPairs<'a> = (Vec<(&'a [Value], Predicate)>, usize);
   798	
   799	fn parse_ifs_pairs<'a>(args: &'a [FnArg], start_index: usize) -> Result<IfsPairs<'a>, ErrorValue> {
   800	    let pair_args = &args[start_index..];
   801	    if pair_args.is_empty() || pair_args.len() % 2 != 0 {
   802	        return Err(ErrorValue::Value);
   803	    }
   804	    let mut pairs: Vec<(&'a [Value], Predicate)> = Vec::with_capacity(pair_args.len() / 2);
   805	    let mut expected: Option<usize> = None;
   806	    let mut chunks = pair_args.chunks_exact(2);
   807	    for pair in chunks.by_ref() {
   808	        let range = match &pair[0] {
   809	            FnArg::Range { values, .. } => values.as_slice(),
   810	            FnArg::Scalar(_) => return Err(ErrorValue::Value),
   811	        };
   812	        let pred_arg = match &pair[1] {
   813	            FnArg::Scalar(v) => v,
   814	            FnArg::Range { .. } => return Err(ErrorValue::Value),
   815	        };
   816	        let pred = build_predicate(pred_arg)?;
   817	        // Shape check.
   818	        match expected {
   819	            None => expected = Some(range.len()),
   820	            Some(n) if n != range.len() => return Err(ErrorValue::Value),
   821	            Some(_) => {}
   822	        }
   823	        pairs.push((range, pred));
   824	    }
   825	    Ok((pairs, expected.unwrap_or(0)))
   826	}
   827	
   828	/// `SUMIFS(sum_range, criteria_range1, criteria1, [crit_range2,
   829	/// crit2, ...])` — sum cells in `sum_range` where ALL
   830	/// criteria_range/criteria pairs match elementwise.
   831	///
   832	/// Excel-canon argument order: sum_range FIRST, then pairs. (SUMIF
   833	/// has sum_range LAST — confusing but it's the spec.)
   834	pub fn sumifs(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
   835	    if args.len() < 3 {
   836	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   837	    }
   838	    let sum_range = match &args[0] {
   839	        FnArg::Range { values, .. } => values.as_slice(),
   840	        FnArg::Scalar(_) => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   841	    };
   842	    let (pairs, expected_len) = match parse_ifs_pairs(args, 1) {
   843	        Ok(x) => x,
   844	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   845	    };
   846	    if sum_range.len() != expected_len {
   847	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   848	    }
   849	    let mut total = 0.0_f64;
   850	    for i in 0..expected_len {
   851	        let mut all_match = true;
   852	        for (range, pred) in &pairs {
   853	            if !pred.matches(&range[i]) {
   854	                all_match = false;
   855	                break;
   856	            }
   857	        }
   858	        if !all_match {
   859	            continue;
   860	        }
   861	        match coerce_numeric(&sum_range[i]) {
   862	            NumericArg::Number(n) => total += n,
   863	            NumericArg::Skip => {}
   864	            NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   865	        }
   866	    }
   867	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(total) {
   868	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   869	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   870	    }
   871	}
   872	
   873	/// `COUNTIFS(criteria_range1, criteria1, [crit_range2, crit2, ...])`
   874	/// — count cells where ALL criteria_range/criteria pairs match
   875	/// elementwise. Errors in range cells don't propagate (Excel canon —
   876	/// same as COUNTIF).
   877	pub fn countifs(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
   878	    if args.is_empty() {
   879	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   880	    }
   881	    let (pairs, expected_len) = match parse_ifs_pairs(args, 0) {
   882	        Ok(x) => x,
   883	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   884	    };
   885	    let mut count: usize = 0;
   886	    for i in 0..expected_len {
   887	        let mut all_match = true;
   888	        for (range, pred) in &pairs {
   889	            if !pred.matches(&range[i]) {
   890	                all_match = false;
   891	                break;
   892	            }
   893	        }
   894	        if all_match {
   895	            count += 1;
   896	        }
   897	    }
   898	    Value::Number(count as f64)
   899	}

   949	/// `SUMPRODUCT(array1, [array2], ...)` — element-wise multiply all
   950	/// arrays and sum the products. All arrays must have the same total
   951	/// length; mismatched → `#VALUE!`. Non-numeric cell values
   952	/// contribute as 0 (Excel canon: SUMPRODUCT is lenient, unlike
   953	/// SUMIF/SUM which propagate text → #VALUE!). Error cells propagate.
   954	pub fn sumproduct(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
   955	    if args.is_empty() {
   956	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   957	    }
   958	    // Collect range slices; scalars in V1 are accepted as 1-element
   959	    // arrays — Excel canon: SUMPRODUCT(5) = 5, SUMPRODUCT(5, range)
   960	    // multiplies every element by 5.
   961	    let mut arrays: Vec<&[Value]> = Vec::with_capacity(args.len());
   962	    let scalar_single: Vec<[Value; 1]>;
   963	    {
   964	        let mut tmp: Vec<[Value; 1]> = Vec::new();
   965	        for a in args {
   966	            match a {
   967	                FnArg::Range { values, .. } => arrays.push(values.as_slice()),
   968	                FnArg::Scalar(v) => {
   969	                    tmp.push([v.clone()]);
   970	                }
   971	            }
   972	        }
   973	        scalar_single = tmp;
   974	    }
   975	    // Push slices for scalar args after collecting (borrow-safe).
   976	    for arr in &scalar_single {
   977	        arrays.push(arr.as_slice());
   978	    }
   979	    // Determine common length: the longest non-singleton array; all
   980	    // non-singletons must agree.
   981	    let mut common_len: Option<usize> = None;
   982	    for arr in &arrays {
   983	        if arr.len() == 1 {
   984	            continue;
   985	        }
   986	        match common_len {
   987	            None => common_len = Some(arr.len()),
   988	            Some(n) if n != arr.len() => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   989	            Some(_) => {}
   990	        }
   991	    }
   992	    let total_len = common_len.unwrap_or(1);
   993	    // Coerce a Value to f64 lenient-for-SUMPRODUCT: Number → n; Bool
   994	    // → 1.0/0.0; Blank → 0; Text → 0 (lenient); Error → propagate.
   995	    let to_num = |v: &Value| -> Result<f64, ErrorValue> {
   996	        match v {
   997	            Value::Number(n) => Ok(*n),
   998	            Value::Boolean(b) => Ok(if *b { 1.0 } else { 0.0 }),
   999	            Value::Blank => Ok(0.0),
  1000	            Value::Text(_) => Ok(0.0),
  1001	            Value::Error(e) => Err(*e),
  1002	        }
  1003	    };
  1004	    let mut sum = 0.0_f64;
  1005	    for i in 0..total_len {
  1006	        let mut prod = 1.0_f64;
  1007	        for arr in &arrays {
  1008	            let idx = if arr.len() == 1 { 0 } else { i };
  1009	            match to_num(&arr[idx]) {
  1010	                Ok(n) => prod *= n,
  1011	                Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1012	            }
  1013	        }
  1014	        sum += prod;
  1015	    }
  1016	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(sum) {
  1017	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
  1018	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
  1019	    }
  1020	}
  1021	
  1022	// ===== W5-58: Stats family (LARGE / SMALL / RANK / MEDIAN / MODE) =====
  1023	//
  1024	// Order-statistic and frequency functions over a range or variadic
  1025	// numeric args. All accept any mix of `Range` and `Scalar` arg
  1026	// shapes — values are flattened via `collect_numbers_strict`.
  1027	//
  1028	// Excel-canon edge cases:
  1029	// - Text/Blank handling: matches the existing scalar-fns SUM /
  1030	//   AVERAGE — Blank is skipped, Text is rejected as #VALUE!.
  1031	//   (Excel's behavior for Text in a range arg differs from a
  1032	//   scalar arg; we use the strict rule throughout for consistency
  1033	//   with the rest of the function library.)
  1034	// - Empty after coercion → #NUM!.
  1035	// - LARGE / SMALL: k < 1 or k > count → #NUM!.
  1036	// - MEDIAN: even count → average of the two middle values.
  1037	// - MODE: returns the most-frequent value; tie-broken by first
  1038	//   appearance. If no value repeats → #N/A.
  1039	
  1040	/// Helper: flatten all numeric values from a list of FnArgs.
  1041	/// Scalar and Range both contribute their values. Blank skipped;
  1042	/// Text → #VALUE!; Error propagates. Booleans coerce 0/1.
  1043	fn collect_numbers_strict(args: &[FnArg]) -> Result<Vec<f64>, ErrorValue> {
  1044	    let mut out = Vec::new();
  1045	    for arg in args {
  1046	        match arg {
  1047	            FnArg::Scalar(v) => match coerce_numeric(v) {
  1048	                NumericArg::Number(n) => out.push(n),
  1049	                NumericArg::Skip => {}
  1050	                NumericArg::Error(e) => return Err(e),
  1051	            },
  1052	            FnArg::Range { values, .. } => {
  1053	                for v in values {
  1054	                    match coerce_numeric(v) {
  1055	                        NumericArg::Number(n) => out.push(n),
  1056	                        NumericArg::Skip => {}
  1057	                        NumericArg::Error(e) => return Err(e),
  1058	                    }
  1059	                }
  1060	            }
  1061	        }
  1062	    }
  1063	    Ok(out)
  1064	}
  1065	
  1066	/// `LARGE(array, k)` — k-th largest value in `array`. 1-based k.
  1067	/// k < 1 or k > count → `#NUM!`. Empty array → `#NUM!`.
  1068	pub fn large(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
  1069	    if args.len() != 2 {
  1070	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1071	    }
  1072	    let arr = match collect_numbers_strict(&args[..1]) {
  1073	        Ok(v) => v,
  1074	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1075	    };
  1076	    let k = match &args[1] {
  1077	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Number(n)) => n.trunc() as i64,
  1078	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Boolean(b)) => i64::from(*b),
  1079	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Blank) => 0,
  1080	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Error(e)) => return Value::Error(*e),
  1081	        _ => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
  1082	    };
  1083	    if arr.is_empty() || k < 1 || (k as usize) > arr.len() {
  1084	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
  1085	    }
  1086	    let mut sorted = arr;
  1087	    // Descending so index k-1 is the k-th largest.
  1088	    sorted.sort_by(|a, b| b.partial_cmp(a).unwrap_or(std::cmp::Ordering::Equal));
  1089	    Value::Number(sorted[(k - 1) as usize])
  1090	}
  1091	
  1092	/// `SMALL(array, k)` — k-th smallest. Same constraints as LARGE.
  1093	pub fn small(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
  1094	    if args.len() != 2 {
  1095	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1096	    }
  1097	    let arr = match collect_numbers_strict(&args[..1]) {
  1098	        Ok(v) => v,
  1099	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1100	    };
  1101	    let k = match &args[1] {
  1102	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Number(n)) => n.trunc() as i64,
  1103	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Boolean(b)) => i64::from(*b),
  1104	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Blank) => 0,
  1105	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Error(e)) => return Value::Error(*e),
  1106	        _ => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
  1107	    };
  1108	    if arr.is_empty() || k < 1 || (k as usize) > arr.len() {
  1109	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
  1110	    }
  1111	    let mut sorted = arr;
  1112	    sorted.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap_or(std::cmp::Ordering::Equal));
  1113	    Value::Number(sorted[(k - 1) as usize])
  1114	}
  1115	
  1116	/// `RANK(value, ref, [order])` — 1-based rank of `value` within
  1117	/// `ref`. order = 0 or omitted = descending (largest = rank 1);
  1118	/// order != 0 = ascending. Ties get the same rank (Excel's RANK.EQ
  1119	/// behavior — the older RANK function is identical in V1 since we
  1120	/// don't ship RANK.AVG separately). value not in ref → `#N/A`.
  1121	pub fn rank(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
  1122	    if args.len() < 2 || args.len() > 3 {
  1123	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1124	    }
  1125	    let target = match &args[0] {
  1126	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Number(n)) => *n,
  1127	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Boolean(b)) => {
  1128	            if *b {
  1129	                1.0
  1130	            } else {
  1131	                0.0
  1132	            }
  1133	        }
  1134	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Blank) => 0.0,
  1135	        FnArg::Scalar(Value::Error(e)) => return Value::Error(*e),
  1136	        _ => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
  1137	    };
  1138	    let arr = match collect_numbers_strict(&args[1..2]) {
  1139	        Ok(v) => v,
  1140	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1141	    };
  1142	    if arr.is_empty() {
  1143	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::NA);
  1144	    }
  1145	    let order = if args.len() == 3 {
  1146	        match &args[2] {
  1147	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Number(n)) => *n != 0.0,
  1148	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Boolean(b)) => *b,
  1149	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Blank) => false,
  1150	            FnArg::Scalar(Value::Error(e)) => return Value::Error(*e),
  1151	            _ => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
  1152	        }
  1153	    } else {
  1154	        false
  1155	    };
  1156	    // Check `value` is in `ref`.
  1157	    if !arr.contains(&target) {
  1158	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::NA);
  1159	    }
  1160	    // Rank: count of values strictly better than `value`, then +1.
  1161	    // Ties get the same rank (smallest in the tie group).
  1162	    let better = if order {
  1163	        // Ascending — rank 1 is the SMALLEST. Better = strictly less.
  1164	        arr.iter().filter(|x| **x < target).count()
  1165	    } else {
  1166	        // Descending — rank 1 is the LARGEST. Better = strictly greater.
  1167	        arr.iter().filter(|x| **x > target).count()
  1168	    };
  1169	    Value::Number((better + 1) as f64)
  1170	}
  1171	
  1172	/// `MEDIAN(num1, num2, ...)` — median of the flattened numeric
  1173	/// values. Even count → average of the two middle. Empty → `#NUM!`.
  1174	pub fn median(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
  1175	    if args.is_empty() {
  1176	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1177	    }
  1178	    let mut nums = match collect_numbers_strict(args) {
  1179	        Ok(v) => v,
  1180	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1181	    };
  1182	    if nums.is_empty() {
  1183	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
  1184	    }
  1185	    nums.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap_or(std::cmp::Ordering::Equal));
  1186	    let len = nums.len();
  1187	    let result = if len % 2 == 1 {
  1188	        nums[len / 2]
  1189	    } else {
  1190	        (nums[len / 2 - 1] + nums[len / 2]) / 2.0
  1191	    };
  1192	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(result) {
  1193	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
  1194	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
  1195	    }
  1196	}
  1197	
  1198	/// `MODE(num1, num2, ...)` — most frequent value. Tie-broken by
  1199	/// first appearance. Returns `#N/A` if no value repeats. Empty →
  1200	/// `#NUM!`. (Modern Excel name: `MODE.SNGL` — registered as an
  1201	/// alias.)
  1202	pub fn mode(args: &[FnArg]) -> Value {
  1203	    if args.is_empty() {
  1204	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1205	    }
  1206	    let nums = match collect_numbers_strict(args) {
  1207	        Ok(v) => v,
  1208	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1209	    };
  1210	    if nums.is_empty() {
  1211	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
  1212	    }
  1213	    // Count frequencies. Bit-pattern key — sanitize_f64 already
  1214	    // filtered NaN/Inf from upstream coercion, so this is safe.
  1215	    use std::collections::HashMap;
  1216	    let mut counts: HashMap<u64, (u32, usize)> = HashMap::new();
  1217	    for (i, &v) in nums.iter().enumerate() {
  1218	        let bits = v.to_bits();
  1219	        counts
  1220	            .entry(bits)
  1221	            .and_modify(|(c, _)| *c += 1)
  1222	            .or_insert((1, i));
  1223	    }
  1224	    // Pick max count; tie-break by smallest first_index.
  1225	    let mut best: Option<(u64, u32, usize)> = None;
  1226	    for (bits, (count, first_idx)) in &counts {
  1227	        if *count < 2 {
  1228	            continue;
  1229	        }
  1230	        match best {
  1231	            None => best = Some((*bits, *count, *first_idx)),
  1232	            Some((_, bc, bi)) => {
  1233	                if *count > bc || (*count == bc && *first_idx < bi) {
  1234	                    best = Some((*bits, *count, *first_idx));
  1235	                }
  1236	            }
  1237	        }
  1238	    }
  1239	    match best {
  1240	        Some((bits, _, _)) => Value::Number(f64::from_bits(bits)),
  1241	        None => Value::Error(ErrorValue::NA),
  1242	    }
  1243	}
