     1	//! Scalar built-in functions for Phase 0.
     2	//!
     3	//! Each function is `fn(&[Value]) -> Value` with Excel-compatible semantics. Args are
     4	//! pre-evaluated by the caller (the binder produces a flat arg list; range references
     5	//! get expanded to per-cell Values before this layer).
     6	//!
     7	//! ## Phase 0 function set (W4-4)
     8	//!
     9	//! Numeric aggregates: SUM, AVERAGE, COUNT, COUNTA, MIN, MAX, PRODUCT, VAR.S, VAR.P,
    10	//! STDEV.S, STDEV.P.
    11	//!
    12	//! Logical: IF, AND, OR, NOT, IFERROR.
    13	//!
    14	//! Math: ABS, SQRT, ROUND, INT, MOD, POWER.
    15	//!
    16	//! Type coercion semantics mirror `ql-types::coercion`:
    17	//! - SUM / AVERAGE / etc. use `to_number_strict` per arg; Text → #VALUE!.
    18	//! - COUNT counts numeric values only (Bool/Text/Error/Blank skipped).
    19	//! - COUNTA counts non-blank values (Error counted).
    20	//! - Error propagation: any Error arg → return that error.
    21	
    22	use ql_types::{coercion, ErrorValue, Value};
    23	
    24	use crate::welford;
    25	use crate::welford::WelfordState;
    26	
    27	/// Helper: extract `f64` from a Value, propagating Error. Skip Blank (treats as
    28	/// "absent" — caller decides whether that's an error or a no-op for the function).
    29	enum NumericArg {
    30	    Number(f64),
    31	    Skip,
    32	    Error(ErrorValue),
    33	}
    34	
    35	fn coerce_numeric(v: &Value) -> NumericArg {
    36	    match v {
    37	        Value::Error(e) => NumericArg::Error(*e),
    38	        Value::Blank => NumericArg::Skip,
    39	        other => match coercion::to_number_strict(other) {
    40	            Ok(n) => NumericArg::Number(n),
    41	            Err(e) => NumericArg::Error(e),
    42	        },
    43	    }
    44	}
    45	
    46	/// Helper: feed args through a Welford-style accumulator (streaming variant).
    47	/// Errors short-circuit. Returns `Ok((state))` if all args usable.
    48	fn welford_from_args(args: &[Value]) -> Result<WelfordState, ErrorValue> {
    49	    let mut state = WelfordState::new();
    50	    for v in args {
    51	        match coerce_numeric(v) {
    52	            NumericArg::Number(n) => state.update(n),
    53	            NumericArg::Skip => {} // Blank skipped — Excel SUM/AVERAGE treats blanks as absent.
    54	            NumericArg::Error(e) => return Err(e),
    55	        }
    56	    }
    57	    Ok(state)
    58	}
    59	
    60	/// Helper: materialize args into a `Vec<f64>` for two-pass batch processing. Errors
    61	/// short-circuit; Blank skipped.
    62	fn numeric_args(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Vec<f64>, ErrorValue> {
    63	    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(args.len());
    64	    for v in args {
    65	        match coerce_numeric(v) {
    66	            NumericArg::Number(n) => out.push(n),
    67	            NumericArg::Skip => {}
    68	            NumericArg::Error(e) => return Err(e),
    69	        }
    70	    }
    71	    Ok(out)
    72	}
    73	
    74	// ===== Aggregates =====
    75	
    76	/// `SUM(args...)` — sum of numeric values; Blank skipped; Error propagates.
    77	pub fn sum(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
    78	    let mut total = 0.0_f64;
    79	    for v in args {
    80	        match coerce_numeric(v) {
   820	//
   821	// CEILING / FLOOR / MROUND / ODD / EVEN / QUOTIENT / GCD / LCM.
   822	// All scalar. Excel rounding-canon sign rules:
   823	// - CEILING / FLOOR / MROUND: if number > 0 and significance < 0,
   824	//   return #NUM!. Same for the reverse (number < 0 with significance
   825	//   > 0 is allowed but rounds TOWARD zero).
   826	// - QUOTIENT: integer truncation toward zero (NOT floor); 0
   827	//   denominator → #DIV/0!.
   828	// - GCD / LCM: all args must be non-negative integers; mixed-sign or
   829	//   non-integer → #NUM!.
   830	
   831	/// `CEILING(number, [significance])` — round `number` UP (away from
   832	/// zero) to the nearest multiple of `significance`. Default
   833	/// significance = 1.
   834	///
   835	/// Sign rule (Excel canon): if `number > 0` and `significance < 0`,
   836	/// returns `#NUM!`. `significance = 0` returns 0.
   837	pub fn ceiling(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   838	    if args.is_empty() || args.len() > 2 {
   839	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   840	    }
   841	    let number = match coerce_numeric(&args[0]) {
   842	        NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
   843	        NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
   844	        NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   845	    };
   846	    let significance = if args.len() == 2 {
   847	        match coerce_numeric(&args[1]) {
   848	            NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
   849	            NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
   850	            NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   851	        }
   852	    } else {
   853	        1.0
   854	    };
   855	    if significance == 0.0 {
   856	        return Value::Number(0.0);
   857	    }
   858	    if number > 0.0 && significance < 0.0 {
   859	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
   860	    }
   861	    match coercion::sanitize_f64((number / significance).ceil() * significance) {
   862	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   863	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   864	    }
   865	}
   866	
   867	/// `FLOOR(number, [significance])` — round `number` DOWN (toward
   868	/// zero) to the nearest multiple of `significance`. Default
   869	/// significance = 1.
   870	///
   871	/// Same sign rule as CEILING.
   872	pub fn floor(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   873	    if args.is_empty() || args.len() > 2 {
   874	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   875	    }
   876	    let number = match coerce_numeric(&args[0]) {
   877	        NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
   878	        NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
   879	        NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   880	    };
   881	    let significance = if args.len() == 2 {
   882	        match coerce_numeric(&args[1]) {
   883	            NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
   884	            NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
   885	            NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   886	        }
   887	    } else {
   888	        1.0
   889	    };
   890	    if significance == 0.0 {
   891	        if number == 0.0 {
   892	            return Value::Number(0.0);
   893	        }
   894	        // Non-zero / 0 → #DIV/0! per Excel FLOOR canon (vs CEILING
   895	        // which returns 0 — yes, the two differ in this edge).
   896	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero);
   897	    }
   898	    if number > 0.0 && significance < 0.0 {
   899	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
   900	    }
   901	    match coercion::sanitize_f64((number / significance).floor() * significance) {
   902	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   903	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   904	    }
   905	}
   906	
   907	/// `MROUND(number, multiple)` — round `number` to the nearest
   908	/// multiple of `multiple`. .5 rounds away from zero (Excel canon).
   909	/// Sign rule: number and multiple must have the same sign; mixed →
   910	/// `#NUM!`. multiple = 0 returns 0.
   911	pub fn mround(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   912	    if args.len() != 2 {
   913	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
   914	    }
   915	    let number = match coerce_numeric(&args[0]) {
   916	        NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
   917	        NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
   918	        NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   919	    };
   920	    let multiple = match coerce_numeric(&args[1]) {
   921	        NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
   922	        NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
   923	        NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   924	    };
   925	    if multiple == 0.0 {
   926	        return Value::Number(0.0);
   927	    }
   928	    if (number > 0.0 && multiple < 0.0) || (number < 0.0 && multiple > 0.0) {
   929	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
   930	    }
   931	    // Round-half-away-from-zero. `(x).round()` already does that
   932	    // in Rust for non-negative; for negative the rounding goes the
   933	    // other way. We use the formula `((x / m + 0.5*sign(x)).trunc()) * m`.
   934	    let q = number / multiple;
   935	    let rounded = if q >= 0.0 {
   936	        (q + 0.5).floor()
   937	    } else {
   938	        (q - 0.5).ceil()
   939	    };
   940	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(rounded * multiple) {
   941	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   942	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   943	    }
   944	}
   945	
   946	/// `ODD(number)` — round AWAY from zero to the nearest odd integer.
   947	/// ODD(0) = 1 per Excel canon.
   948	pub fn odd(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   949	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
   950	        Ok(n) => n,
   951	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   952	    };
   953	    if n == 0.0 {
   954	        return Value::Number(1.0);
   955	    }
   956	    // Round away from zero to the next integer of the right parity.
   957	    let away = if n > 0.0 { n.ceil() } else { n.floor() };
   958	    let away_i = away as i64;
   959	    // If even, bump by ±1 to get to odd.
   960	    let adjusted = if away_i % 2 == 0 {
   961	        if n > 0.0 {
   962	            away_i + 1
   963	        } else {
   964	            away_i - 1
   965	        }
   966	    } else {
   967	        away_i
   968	    };
   969	    Value::Number(adjusted as f64)
   970	}
   971	
   972	/// `EVEN(number)` — round AWAY from zero to the nearest even
   973	/// integer. EVEN(0) = 0.
   974	pub fn even(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   975	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
   976	        Ok(n) => n,
   977	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   978	    };
   979	    if n == 0.0 {
   980	        return Value::Number(0.0);
   981	    }
   982	    let away = if n > 0.0 { n.ceil() } else { n.floor() };
   983	    let away_i = away as i64;
   984	    let adjusted = if away_i % 2 != 0 {
   985	        if n > 0.0 {
   986	            away_i + 1
   987	        } else {
   988	            away_i - 1
   989	        }
   990	    } else {
   991	        away_i
   992	    };
   993	    Value::Number(adjusted as f64)
   994	}
   995	
   996	/// `QUOTIENT(numerator, denominator)` — integer quotient, truncated
   997	/// TOWARD ZERO (not floored). `denominator = 0` → `#DIV/0!`.
   998	pub fn quotient(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   999	    if args.len() != 2 {
  1000	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1001	    }
  1002	    let num = match coerce_numeric(&args[0]) {
  1003	        NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
  1004	        NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
  1005	        NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1006	    };
  1007	    let den = match coerce_numeric(&args[1]) {
  1008	        NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
  1009	        NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
  1010	        NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1011	    };
  1012	    if den == 0.0 {
  1013	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero);
  1014	    }
  1015	    Value::Number((num / den).trunc())
  1016	}
  1017	
  1018	/// Helper for GCD / LCM: collect args as non-negative integers.
  1019	/// Variadic; rejects mixed signs, non-integers, errors.
  1020	fn collect_nonneg_integers(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Vec<u64>, ErrorValue> {
  1021	    if args.is_empty() {
  1022	        return Err(ErrorValue::Value);
  1023	    }
  1024	    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(args.len());
  1025	    for a in args {
  1026	        let n = match coerce_numeric(a) {
  1027	            NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
  1028	            NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
  1029	            NumericArg::Error(e) => return Err(e),
  1030	        };
  1031	        if n < 0.0 {
  1032	            return Err(ErrorValue::Num);
  1033	        }
  1034	        let truncated = n.trunc();
  1035	        // Excel: GCD/LCM truncate to integer per docs (not error).
  1036	        // Bound for safety.
  1037	        if !truncated.is_finite() || truncated > (u64::MAX as f64) {
  1038	            return Err(ErrorValue::Num);
  1039	        }
  1040	        out.push(truncated as u64);
  1041	    }
  1042	    Ok(out)
  1043	}
  1044	
  1045	fn gcd2(a: u64, b: u64) -> u64 {
  1046	    let (mut a, mut b) = (a, b);
  1047	    while b != 0 {
  1048	        let t = b;
  1049	        b = a % b;
  1050	        a = t;
  1051	    }
  1052	    a
  1053	}
  1054	
  1055	/// `GCD(num1, num2, ...)` — greatest common divisor. Variadic.
  1056	/// All args must be non-negative; mixed sign / negative → `#NUM!`.
  1057	/// Excel: `GCD(0, 0, ..., 0) = 0`.
  1058	pub fn gcd(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1059	    let nums = match collect_nonneg_integers(args) {
  1060	        Ok(v) => v,
  1061	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1062	    };
  1063	    let mut g: u64 = 0;
  1064	    for n in nums {
  1065	        g = gcd2(g, n);
  1066	    }
  1067	    Value::Number(g as f64)
  1068	}
  1069	
  1070	/// `LCM(num1, num2, ...)` — least common multiple. Variadic.
  1071	/// All args must be non-negative integers; mixed sign / negative →
  1072	/// `#NUM!`. LCM with any 0 returns 0.
  1073	pub fn lcm(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1074	    let nums = match collect_nonneg_integers(args) {
  1075	        Ok(v) => v,
  1076	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1077	    };
  1078	    // LCM identity is 1; but if any arg is 0, result is 0.
  1079	    if nums.contains(&0) {
  1080	        return Value::Number(0.0);
  1081	    }
  1082	    let mut result: u128 = 1;
  1083	    for n in nums {
  1084	        let n_u128 = n as u128;
  1085	        let g = gcd2(result as u64, n) as u128;
  1086	        // result = (result * n) / gcd. Use u128 to defer overflow.
  1087	        match result.checked_mul(n_u128 / g) {
  1088	            Some(v) => result = v,
  1089	            None => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num),
  1090	        }
  1091	    }
  1092	    if result > (u64::MAX as u128) || (result as f64) > f64::MAX {
  1093	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
  1094	    }
  1095	    Value::Number(result as f64)
  1096	}
  1097	
  1098	// ===== Hyperbolic trig (W5-57, Phase 4.3 V2) =====
  1099	
  1100	/// `SINH(number)` — hyperbolic sine. Overflow → #NUM! via
  1101	/// `sanitize_f64`.
  1102	pub fn sinh(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1103	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
  1104	        Ok(n) => n,
  1105	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1106	    };
  1107	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.sinh()) {
  1108	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
  1109	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
  1110	    }
  1111	}
  1112	
  1113	/// `COSH(number)` — hyperbolic cosine. Overflow → #NUM!.
  1114	pub fn cosh(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1115	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
  1116	        Ok(n) => n,
  1117	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1118	    };
  1119	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.cosh()) {
  1120	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
  1121	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
  1122	    }
  1123	}
  1124	
  1125	/// `TANH(number)` — hyperbolic tangent. Saturates to ±1 for large
  1126	/// |n| (no overflow). Total domain.
  1127	pub fn tanh(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1128	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
  1129	        Ok(n) => n,
  1130	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1131	    };
  1132	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.tanh()) {
  1133	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
  1134	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
  1135	    }
  1136	}
  1137	
  1138	/// `ASINH(number)` — inverse hyperbolic sine. Total real domain.
  1139	pub fn asinh(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1140	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
  1141	        Ok(n) => n,
  1142	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1143	    };
  1144	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.asinh()) {
  1145	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
  1146	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
  1147	    }
  1148	}
  1149	
  1150	/// `ACOSH(number)` — inverse hyperbolic cosine. Domain `n >= 1`;
  1151	/// out-of-domain → `#NUM!`.
  1152	pub fn acosh(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1153	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
  1154	        Ok(n) => n,
  1155	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1156	    };
  1157	    if n < 1.0 {
  1158	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
  1159	    }
  1160	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.acosh()) {
  1161	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
  1162	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
  1163	    }
  1164	}
  1165	
  1166	/// `ATANH(number)` — inverse hyperbolic tangent. Domain `|n| < 1`;
  1167	/// out-of-domain → `#NUM!`. (Excel canon: ATANH(±1) is undefined and
  1168	/// returns #NUM! rather than ±∞.)
  1169	pub fn atanh(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1170	    let n = match one_number(args, 1, 1) {
  1171	        Ok(n) => n,
  1172	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1173	    };
  1174	    if !(n > -1.0 && n < 1.0) {
  1175	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
  1176	    }
  1177	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(n.atanh()) {
  1178	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
  1179	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
  1180	    }
  1181	}
  1182	
  1183	/// `ATAN2(x_num, y_num)` — two-argument arc tangent. Returns
  1184	/// radians in `(-π, π]`. Excel takes the angle's x-coordinate FIRST,
  1185	/// then y — the inverse of Rust's `f64::atan2(y, x)`. Per IronCalc +
  1186	/// the W3C / OOXML reference, Quantbook takes `args[0]` as `x` and
  1187	/// `args[1]` as `y` and internally calls `y.atan2(x)`. Returns
  1188	/// `#DIV/0!` if both args are zero (Excel canon; Rust's
  1189	/// `f64::atan2(0.0, 0.0)` would silently return 0.0). The W5-52
  1190	/// audit closure fixed this docstring's title — it previously said
  1191	/// `ATAN2(y, x)`, inverting the labels.
  1192	pub fn atan2(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1193	    if args.len() != 2 {
  1194	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1195	    }
  1196	    let x = match coerce_numeric(&args[0]) {
  1197	        NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
  1198	        NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
  1199	        NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1200	    };
  1201	    let y = match coerce_numeric(&args[1]) {
  1202	        NumericArg::Number(n) => n,
  1203	        NumericArg::Skip => 0.0,
  1204	        NumericArg::Error(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1205	    };
  1206	    if x == 0.0 && y == 0.0 {
  1207	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero);
  1208	    }
  1209	    // Excel arg order is (x, y); Rust's f64::atan2 is (y, x).
  1210	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(y.atan2(x)) {
  1211	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
  1212	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
  1213	    }
  1214	}
  1215	
  1216	// ===== Text (Phase 4.3 V1) =====
  1217	
  1218	/// Helper: coerce a Value to its display string per Excel canon.
  1219	/// Numbers print as their f64 representation; Bool as TRUE/FALSE;
  1220	/// Text passes through; Blank as "" (empty string). Errors propagate.
  1221	fn coerce_text(v: &Value) -> Result<String, ErrorValue> {
  1222	    match v {
  1223	        Value::Error(e) => Err(*e),
  1224	        Value::Text(s) => Ok(s.as_ref().to_owned()),
  1225	        Value::Number(n) => Ok(format_number_for_text(*n)),
  1330	            Err(_) => Err(ErrorValue::Value),
  1331	        },
  1332	        Value::Error(e) => Err(*e),
  1333	    }
  1334	}
  1335	
  1336	/// `LEFT(text, [num_chars])` — leftmost `num_chars` characters of
  1337	/// `text`. Default num_chars=1. Negative → #VALUE!. num_chars
  1338	/// greater than text length returns the whole text. Empty text
  1339	/// returns empty.
  1340	pub fn left(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1341	    if args.is_empty() || args.len() > 2 {
  1342	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1343	    }
  1344	    let text = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
  1345	        Ok(s) => s,
  1346	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1347	    };
  1348	    let n = if args.len() == 2 {
  1349	        match coerce_int_arg(&args[1]) {
  1350	            Ok(n) => n,
  1351	            Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1352	        }
  1353	    } else {
  1354	        1
  1355	    };
  1356	    if n < 0 {
  1357	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1358	    }
  1359	    let out: String = text.chars().take(n as usize).collect();
  1360	    Value::text(out)
  1361	}
  1362	
  1363	/// `RIGHT(text, [num_chars])` — rightmost `num_chars` characters.
  1364	/// Same caveats as LEFT.
  1365	pub fn right(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1366	    if args.is_empty() || args.len() > 2 {
  1367	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1368	    }
  1369	    let text = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
  1370	        Ok(s) => s,
  1371	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1372	    };
  1373	    let n = if args.len() == 2 {
  1374	        match coerce_int_arg(&args[1]) {
  1375	            Ok(n) => n,
  1376	            Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1377	        }
  1378	    } else {
  1379	        1
  1380	    };
  1381	    if n < 0 {
  1382	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1383	    }
  1384	    let total = text.chars().count();
  1385	    let take_from = total.saturating_sub(n as usize);
  1386	    let out: String = text.chars().skip(take_from).collect();
  1387	    Value::text(out)
  1388	}
  1389	
  1390	/// `MID(text, start_num, num_chars)` — substring starting at
  1391	/// `start_num` (1-based) of length `num_chars`. start_num < 1 →
  1392	/// #VALUE!; num_chars < 0 → #VALUE!. start_num past end → empty.
  1393	pub fn mid(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1394	    if args.len() != 3 {
  1395	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1396	    }
  1397	    let text = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
  1398	        Ok(s) => s,
  1399	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1400	    };
  1401	    let start = match coerce_int_arg(&args[1]) {
  1402	        Ok(n) => n,
  1403	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1404	    };
  1405	    let len = match coerce_int_arg(&args[2]) {
  1406	        Ok(n) => n,
  1407	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1408	    };
  1409	    if start < 1 || len < 0 {
  1410	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1411	    }
  1412	    let skip = (start - 1) as usize;
  1413	    let take = len as usize;
  1414	    let out: String = text.chars().skip(skip).take(take).collect();
  1415	    Value::text(out)
  1416	}
  1417	
  1418	/// `FIND(find_text, within_text, [start_num])` — 1-based position
  1419	/// of `find_text` inside `within_text`, starting search at
  1420	/// `start_num` (default 1). **Case-sensitive.** Not found → #VALUE!.
  1421	/// start_num < 1 or > length → #VALUE!. No wildcard support.
  1422	pub fn find(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1423	    if args.len() < 2 || args.len() > 3 {
  1424	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1425	    }
  1426	    let needle = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
  1427	        Ok(s) => s,
  1428	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1429	    };
  1430	    let hay = match coerce_text(&args[1]) {
  1431	        Ok(s) => s,
  1432	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1433	    };
  1434	    let start = if args.len() == 3 {
  1435	        match coerce_int_arg(&args[2]) {
  1436	            Ok(n) => n,
  1437	            Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1438	        }
  1439	    } else {
  1440	        1
  1441	    };
  1442	    let total = hay.chars().count();
  1443	    if start < 1 || (start as usize) > total + 1 {
  1444	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1445	    }
  1446	    let start_idx = (start - 1) as usize;
  1447	    // Collect chars to allow indexing by char position.
  1448	    let chars: Vec<char> = hay.chars().collect();
  1449	    let needle_chars: Vec<char> = needle.chars().collect();
  1450	    if needle_chars.is_empty() {
  1451	        // Empty needle matches at start_num per Excel canon.
  1452	        return Value::Number(start as f64);
  1453	    }
  1454	    let n_len = needle_chars.len();
  1455	    let h_len = chars.len();
  1456	    if start_idx + n_len > h_len {
  1457	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1458	    }
  1459	    for i in start_idx..=(h_len - n_len) {
  1460	        if chars[i..i + n_len] == needle_chars[..] {
  1461	            return Value::Number((i + 1) as f64);
  1462	        }
  1463	    }
  1464	    Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value)
  1465	}
  1466	
  1467	/// `SEARCH(find_text, within_text, [start_num])` — like FIND but
  1468	/// **case-insensitive**. Excel also supports `?` (single char) and
  1469	/// `*` (any chars) wildcards — V1 does NOT implement wildcards;
  1470	/// they're documented as deferred in the matrix and treated as
  1471	/// literal characters here.
  1472	pub fn search(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1473	    if args.len() < 2 || args.len() > 3 {
  1474	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1475	    }
  1476	    let needle = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
  1477	        Ok(s) => s,
  1478	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1479	    };
  1480	    let hay = match coerce_text(&args[1]) {
  1481	        Ok(s) => s,
  1482	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1483	    };
  1484	    let start = if args.len() == 3 {
  1485	        match coerce_int_arg(&args[2]) {
  1486	            Ok(n) => n,
  1487	            Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1488	        }
  1489	    } else {
  1490	        1
  1491	    };
  1492	    let total = hay.chars().count();
  1493	    if start < 1 || (start as usize) > total + 1 {
  1494	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1495	    }
  1496	    let start_idx = (start - 1) as usize;
  1497	    let needle_u: Vec<char> = needle.to_uppercase().chars().collect();
  1498	    let hay_u: Vec<char> = hay.to_uppercase().chars().collect();
  1499	    if needle_u.is_empty() {
  1500	        return Value::Number(start as f64);
  1501	    }
  1502	    let n_len = needle_u.len();
  1503	    let h_len = hay_u.len();
  1504	    if start_idx + n_len > h_len {
  1505	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1506	    }
  1507	    for i in start_idx..=(h_len - n_len) {
  1508	        if hay_u[i..i + n_len] == needle_u[..] {
  1509	            return Value::Number((i + 1) as f64);
  1510	        }
  1511	    }
  1512	    Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value)
  1513	}
  1514	
  1515	/// `SUBSTITUTE(text, old_text, new_text, [instance_num])` —
  1516	/// replace `old_text` with `new_text` inside `text`. If
  1517	/// `instance_num` is omitted, replaces all occurrences; otherwise
  1518	/// replaces only the Nth (1-based) occurrence. Case-sensitive.
  1519	pub fn substitute(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1520	    if args.len() < 3 || args.len() > 4 {
  1521	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1522	    }
  1523	    let text = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
  1524	        Ok(s) => s,
  1525	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1526	    };
  1527	    let old = match coerce_text(&args[1]) {
  1528	        Ok(s) => s,
  1529	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1530	    };
  1531	    let new_t = match coerce_text(&args[2]) {
  1532	        Ok(s) => s,
  1533	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1534	    };
  1535	    // Empty `old` is a no-op (Excel returns the text unchanged).
  1536	    if old.is_empty() {
  1537	        return Value::text(text);
  1538	    }
  1539	    let instance: Option<i64> = if args.len() == 4 {
  1540	        match coerce_int_arg(&args[3]) {
  1541	            Ok(n) if n >= 1 => Some(n),
  1542	            Ok(_) => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
  1543	            Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1544	        }
  1545	    } else {
  1546	        None
  1547	    };
  1548	    match instance {
  1549	        None => {
  1550	            // Replace all.
  1551	            Value::text(text.replace(&old, &new_t))
  1552	        }
  1553	        Some(target) => {
  1554	            // Replace only the Nth occurrence.
  1555	            let mut out = String::with_capacity(text.len());
  1556	            let mut remainder = text.as_str();
  1557	            let mut count: i64 = 0;
  1558	            while let Some(idx) = remainder.find(&old) {
  1559	                count += 1;
  1560	                if count == target {
  1561	                    out.push_str(&remainder[..idx]);
  1562	                    out.push_str(&new_t);
  1563	                    out.push_str(&remainder[idx + old.len()..]);
  1564	                    return Value::text(out);
  1565	                }
  1566	                // Copy past this occurrence unchanged.
  1567	                out.push_str(&remainder[..idx + old.len()]);
  1568	                remainder = &remainder[idx + old.len()..];
  1569	            }
  1570	            // Target instance not found — return original text.
  1571	            out.push_str(remainder);
  1572	            Value::text(out)
  1573	        }
  1574	    }
  1575	}
  1576	
  1577	/// `REPLACE(old_text, start_num, num_chars, new_text)` — replace
  1578	/// the `num_chars` characters starting at 1-based `start_num` in
  1579	/// `old_text` with `new_text`. Excel canon: start_num past end
  1580	/// appends; num_chars > remaining length truncates to end. start_num
  1581	/// < 1 or num_chars < 0 → #VALUE!.
  1582	pub fn replace_fn(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1583	    if args.len() != 4 {
  1584	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1585	    }
  1586	    let text = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
  1587	        Ok(s) => s,
  1588	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1589	    };
  1590	    let start = match coerce_int_arg(&args[1]) {
  1591	        Ok(n) => n,
  1592	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1593	    };
  1594	    let cnt = match coerce_int_arg(&args[2]) {
  1595	        Ok(n) => n,
  1596	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1597	    };
  1598	    let new_t = match coerce_text(&args[3]) {
  1599	        Ok(s) => s,
  1600	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1601	    };
  1602	    if start < 1 || cnt < 0 {
  1603	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1604	    }
  1605	    let chars: Vec<char> = text.chars().collect();
  1606	    let total = chars.len();
  1607	    let start_idx = (start - 1) as usize;
  1608	    let mut out = String::new();
  1609	    // Prefix: 0..min(start_idx, total).
  1610	    let prefix_end = start_idx.min(total);
  1611	    out.extend(chars[..prefix_end].iter());
  1612	    // Inserted text.
  1613	    out.push_str(&new_t);
  1614	    // Suffix: chars after the removed slice (start_idx + cnt).
  1615	    let suffix_start = (start_idx + cnt as usize).min(total);
  1616	    out.extend(chars[suffix_start..].iter());
  1617	    Value::text(out)
  1618	}
  1619	
  1620	/// `CONCATENATE(text1, text2, ...)` — concatenate scalar text args.
  1621	/// Variadic; needs at least 1 arg. Errors propagate. Numbers coerce
  1622	/// to their text representation.
  1623	pub fn concatenate(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1624	    if args.is_empty() {
  1625	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1626	    }
  1627	    let mut out = String::new();
  1628	    for a in args {
  1629	        match coerce_text(a) {
  1630	            Ok(s) => out.push_str(&s),
  1631	            Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1632	        }
  1633	    }
  1634	    Value::text(out)
  1635	}
  1636	
  1637	/// `REPT(text, num_times)` — repeat `text` `num_times` times.
  1638	/// num_times < 0 → #VALUE!. num_times = 0 → empty.
  1639	pub fn rept(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1640	    if args.len() != 2 {
  1641	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1642	    }
  1643	    let text = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
  1644	        Ok(s) => s,
  1645	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1646	    };
  1647	    let n = match coerce_int_arg(&args[1]) {
  1648	        Ok(n) => n,
  1649	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1650	    };
  1651	    if n < 0 {
  1652	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1653	    }
  1654	    // Guard against pathological inputs.
  1655	    let target_len = text.len().saturating_mul(n as usize);
  1656	    if target_len > 32_767 {
  1657	        // Excel REPT length cap is 32,767 chars; clamp + return #VALUE!
  1658	        // matching the canonical limit.
  1659	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1660	    }
  1661	    Value::text(text.repeat(n as usize))
  1662	}
  1663	
  1664	/// `EXACT(text1, text2)` — case-sensitive equality. Returns TRUE/
  1665	/// FALSE. Numbers coerce to text first.
  1666	pub fn exact(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1667	    if args.len() != 2 {
  1668	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1669	    }
  1670	    let a = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
  1671	        Ok(s) => s,
  1672	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1673	    };
  1674	    let b = match coerce_text(&args[1]) {
  1675	        Ok(s) => s,
  1676	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1677	    };
  1678	    Value::Boolean(a == b)
  1679	}
  1680

  1218	/// Helper: coerce a Value to its display string per Excel canon.
  1219	/// Numbers print as their f64 representation; Bool as TRUE/FALSE;
  1220	/// Text passes through; Blank as "" (empty string). Errors propagate.
  1221	fn coerce_text(v: &Value) -> Result<String, ErrorValue> {
  1222	    match v {
  1223	        Value::Error(e) => Err(*e),
  1224	        Value::Text(s) => Ok(s.as_ref().to_owned()),
  1225	        Value::Number(n) => Ok(format_number_for_text(*n)),
  1226	        Value::Boolean(b) => Ok(if *b { "TRUE" } else { "FALSE" }.to_owned()),
  1227	        Value::Blank => Ok(String::new()),
  1228	    }
  1229	}
  1230	
  1231	/// Excel-canon number → text. Integers render without a trailing `.0`;
  1232	/// otherwise the default `f64` Display is good enough for V1. Phase 4.5
  1233	/// (number formats) replaces this with locale-aware formatting.
  1234	fn format_number_for_text(n: f64) -> String {
  1235	    if n == n.trunc() && n.abs() < 1e15 {
  1236	        format!("{}", n as i64)
  1237	    } else {
  1238	        format!("{n}")
  1239	    }
  1240	}

  1280	/// `TRIM(text)` — strip leading/trailing whitespace AND collapse internal
  1281	/// runs of multiple spaces to a single space. Excel's canon collapses
  1282	/// only standard space (0x20) runs; we follow that.
  1283	pub fn trim(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
  1284	    if args.len() != 1 {
  1285	        return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value);
  1286	    }
  1287	    let text = match coerce_text(&args[0]) {
  1288	        Ok(s) => s,
  1289	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
  1290	    };
  1291	    let mut out = String::with_capacity(text.len());
  1292	    let mut prev_space = false;
  1293	    let trimmed = text.trim_matches(' ');
  1294	    for c in trimmed.chars() {
  1295	        if c == ' ' {
  1296	            if !prev_space {
  1297	                out.push(' ');
  1298	            }
  1299	            prev_space = true;
  1300	        } else {
  1301	            out.push(c);
  1302	            prev_space = false;
  1303	        }
  1304	    }
  1305	    Value::text(out)
  1306	}

  1320	/// Helper: coerce a Value to an integer arg for position/length
  1321	/// fields in text functions. Numbers truncate; Bool 1/0; Blank 0;
  1322	/// Text rejected as #VALUE!; Error propagates.
  1323	fn coerce_int_arg(v: &Value) -> Result<i64, ErrorValue> {
  1324	    match v {
  1325	        Value::Number(n) => Ok(n.trunc() as i64),
  1326	        Value::Boolean(b) => Ok(if *b { 1 } else { 0 }),
  1327	        Value::Blank => Ok(0),

  1330	            Err(_) => Err(ErrorValue::Value),
  1331	        },
  1332	        Value::Error(e) => Err(*e),
  1333	    }
  1334	}

     1	//! Cell-read environment for the scalar evaluator.
     2	//!
     3	//! The evaluator needs to look up cell values when it encounters `ExprPlan::CellRef`.
     4	//! Rather than coupling to `ql-storage::Workbook` directly, we abstract via a trait so:
     5	//!
     6	//! - Test harnesses can supply a `HashMap`-backed fake environment.
     7	//! - The Week 4+ runtime can wrap a real `Workbook` + `ColumnStore` chain.
     8	//! - Future cross-workbook references (Phase 6+) can implement the trait against an
     9	//!   external dataset adapter.
    10	//!
    11	//! The trait is intentionally narrow: just `read_cell`. Sheet-/column-bulk reads belong on

    13	//! it reads Arrow chunks via `ColumnStore::iter_chunks` for batch processing.

    20	/// Read a single cell value. Out-of-bounds reads return `Value::Blank` per Excel semantics.
    21	pub trait CellEnv {
    22	    fn read_cell(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Value;
    23	
    24	    /// Phase 3.6 (2026-05-12) — AGG-3-04 entry point. Materialize a range
    25	    /// into a flat `Vec<Value>` for aggregate functions (SUM, AVERAGE,
    26	    /// MIN, MAX, COUNT, PRODUCT). Default impl iterates the range row by
    27	    /// row using `read_cell`; impls that have cheaper bounds info (e.g.
    28	    /// `WorkbookEnv` clamps to `Sheet::bounds`) override.
    29	    ///
    30	    /// Returns an empty Vec for a degenerate range (e.g. range entirely
    31	    /// outside a sheet's populated area). The aggregate function then
    32	    /// applies its empty-input rule (MIN/MAX → 0; AVERAGE → `#DIV/0!`;
    33	    /// SUM/COUNT/PRODUCT → 0 or 1; see `ql-functions::scalar_fns`).
    34	    fn read_range(&self, range: Range) -> Vec<Value> {
    35	        let mut out = Vec::new();
    36	        for row in range.start_row..=range.end_row {
    37	            for col in range.start_col..=range.end_col {
    38	                out.push(self.read_cell(range.sheet, row, col));
    39	            }
    40	            // Safety: end_row could be `RowId::MAX` (whole-column); the
    41	            // outer range syntax must be bounded by the caller. The
    42	            // default impl iterates to completion — the WorkbookEnv
    43	            // override clamps to `Sheet::bounds().row_extent`.
    44	        }
    45	        out
    46	    }
    47	
    48	    /// **W5-54 (range-aware functions, GAP-F-05 follow-up):** return
    49	    /// the flat `Vec<Value>` for the range PLUS its 2D shape (rows,
    50	    /// cols). For range-aware functions like VLOOKUP/INDEX which
    51	    /// need to address by `(row, col)`, the dispatcher uses this to
    52	    /// construct `FnArg::Range { values, rows, cols }`.
    53	    ///
    54	    /// `rows * cols == values.len()` is the invariant. If the env
    55	    /// clamps to sheet bounds (`WorkbookEnv`), the returned shape
    56	    /// reflects the clamped dimensions. The default impl below
    57	    /// returns the unclamped shape derived from the range bounds —
    58	    /// matching the unclamped `read_range` default.
    59	    fn read_range_with_shape(&self, range: Range) -> (Vec<Value>, usize, usize) {
    60	        let values = self.read_range(range);
    61	        let rows = (range.end_row - range.start_row + 1) as usize;
    62	        let cols = (range.end_col - range.start_col + 1) as usize;
    63	        debug_assert_eq!(rows.saturating_mul(cols), values.len());
    64	        (values, rows, cols)
    65	    }
    66	}

    80	impl<'w> CellEnv for WorkbookEnv<'w> {
    81	    fn read_cell(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Value {
    82	        // Phase 2A.7 audit H6 (2026-05-12): out-of-bounds sheet now surfaces
    83	        // `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref)` — Excel's canonical `#REF!`. Prior
    84	        // behavior silently mapped missing-sheet to `Value::Blank`, which
    85	        // hid stale-sheet refs in saved formulas (e.g., a formula authored
    86	        // against Sheet3 in a workbook later truncated to 2 sheets). Two
    87	        // megaudit agents flagged the silent fallback. Within an existing
    88	        // sheet, missing cells still return `Blank` — that's correct Excel
    89	        // canon for "empty cell."
    90	        match self.workbook.sheet(sheet) {
    91	            Some(s) => s.read(row, col),
    92	            None => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref),
    93	        }
    94	    }
    95	
    96	    /// Phase 3.6 override: clamps the iteration to `Sheet::bounds` so a
    97	    /// `SUM(A:A)` named range with `end_row = RowId::MAX` reads only the
    98	    /// populated rows (typically a few hundred thousand at most on a
    99	    /// real sheet), not all 4 billion `RowId` slots. Without this clamp
   100	    /// AGG-3-04 would correctness-pass but AGG-3-03 (full-column-still-
   101	    /// usable) would hang at evaluation time.
   102	    fn read_range(&self, range: Range) -> Vec<Value> {
   103	        let Some(sheet) = self.workbook.sheet(range.sheet) else {
   104	            return vec![Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref)];
   105	        };
   106	        let bounds = sheet.bounds();
   107	        // bounds extents are "one past max"; convert to inclusive bounds.
   108	        if bounds.row_extent == 0 || bounds.col_extent == 0 {
   109	            return Vec::new();
   110	        }
   111	        let max_row = bounds.row_extent - 1;
   112	        let max_col = bounds.col_extent - 1;
   113	        let end_row = range.end_row.min(max_row);
   114	        let end_col = range.end_col.min(max_col);
   115	        if range.start_row > end_row || range.start_col > end_col {
   116	            return Vec::new();
   117	        }
   118	        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(
   119	            ((end_row - range.start_row + 1) as usize)
   120	                .saturating_mul((end_col - range.start_col + 1) as usize),
   121	        );
   122	        for row in range.start_row..=end_row {
   123	            for col in range.start_col..=end_col {
   124	                out.push(sheet.read(row, col));
   125	            }
   126	        }
   127	        out
   128	    }
   129	
   130	    /// W5-54: clamp-aware shape. Returns the (rows, cols) actually
   131	    /// materialized into the values Vec, NOT the requested-but-
   132	    /// unclamped range dimensions.
   133	    fn read_range_with_shape(&self, range: Range) -> (Vec<Value>, usize, usize) {
   134	        let Some(sheet) = self.workbook.sheet(range.sheet) else {
   135	            return (vec![Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref)], 1, 1);
   136	        };
   137	        let bounds = sheet.bounds();
   138	        if bounds.row_extent == 0 || bounds.col_extent == 0 {
   139	            return (Vec::new(), 0, 0);
   140	        }
   141	        let max_row = bounds.row_extent - 1;
   142	        let max_col = bounds.col_extent - 1;
   143	        let end_row = range.end_row.min(max_row);
   144	        let end_col = range.end_col.min(max_col);
   145	        if range.start_row > end_row || range.start_col > end_col {
   146	            return (Vec::new(), 0, 0);
   147	        }
   148	        let rows = (end_row - range.start_row + 1) as usize;
   149	        let cols = (end_col - range.start_col + 1) as usize;
   150	        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(rows.saturating_mul(cols));
   151	        for row in range.start_row..=end_row {
   152	            for col in range.start_col..=end_col {
   153	                out.push(sheet.read(row, col));
   154	            }
   155	        }
   156	        (out, rows, cols)
   157	    }
   158	}

   448	    ) {
   449	        // First, evict any prior deps for this formula. Required so
   450	        // re-binding a formula whose text changed (e.g. `=A1` → `=B1`)
   451	        // doesn't leave the old cell-dep stamped on the session.
   452	        self.remove_formula_deps(formula_node);
   453	        // W5-50 (GAP-G-01 closure): also revoke prior graph edges +
   454	        // stripe registrations. Without this, stale forward edges
   455	        // create false `#CIRC!` cycles (megaudit H3) and stale stripe
   456	        // entries falsely dirty the formula on writes to the OLD range
   457	        // (megaudit H4). Idempotent on first-time bind.
   458	        self.graph.clear_outgoing(formula_node);
   459	        self.graph.clear_range_deps_for_formula(formula_node);
   460	
   461	        // Walk and collect.
   462	        let mut deps = FormulaDeps::default();
   463	        walk_plan_for_deps(plan, &mut deps);
   464	
   465	        // Register: deduplicate (the walker emits duplicates if the
   466	        // formula references the same cell twice; the session stores
   467	        // each cell once).
   468	        let mut seen_cells: HashSet<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)> = HashSet::new();
   469	        deps.cells.retain(|c| seen_cells.insert(*c));
   470	        let mut seen_names: HashSet<Arc<str>> = HashSet::new();
   471	        deps.names.retain(|n| seen_names.insert(Arc::clone(n)));
   472	        // named_ranges may contain the same name twice — that's
   473	        // legitimate (`SUM(Sales) + AVERAGE(Sales)` references the
   474	        // same range from two arg positions) — leave as-is.
   475	
   476	        // Phase 3.3: register direct cell deps on the session-side
   477	        // reverse index. We do NOT create CellNodes for dep cells (no
   478	        // need — the lookup at `on_set_value` is by (sheet, row, col)
   479	        // tuple, not by NodeId).
   480	        //
   481	        // Phase 3.4: ALSO add a forward `graph.add_edge(formula_node,
   482	        // dep_node)` when the dep cell is ITSELF a formula cell. The
   483	        // Tarjan scheduler walks `graph.outgoing(formula_node)` to
   484	        // discover the dep set; without this edge it can't order a
   485	        // chain like `=A1`, `=B1=A1+1`, `=C1=B1+1` correctly.
   486	        //
   487	        // **W5-50 / W5-52 closure (GAP-G-01):** the original Phase 0
   488	        // append-only contract HAS been relaxed. Re-bind now revokes
   489	        // stale edges via `Graph::clear_outgoing` called from
   490	        // `extract_and_register_deps` entry (line 453) and from
   491	        // `on_clear_formula` (W5-52 audit closure). Stale-edge false
   492	        // `#CIRC!` cycles can no longer occur. See
   493	        // `docs/architecture/2026-05-13-graph-storage-decision.md`.
   494	        for &(s, r, c) in &deps.cells {
   495	            self.cell_to_formulas
   496	                .entry((s, r, c))
   497	                .or_default()
   498	                .insert(formula_node);
   499	            if let Some(&dep_node) = self.cell_index.get(&(s, r, c)) {
   500	                // Note: self-edges (formula_node == dep_node) are
   501	                // ALLOWED here — they're the canonical way to model
   502	                // `=A1` at A1 (self-cycle), and the Phase 0 W3-3
   503	                // Tarjan scheduler treats a self-looped single-node
   504	                // SCC as `cycled`.
   505	                self.graph.add_edge(formula_node, dep_node);
   506	            }
   507	        }
   508	        // Phase 3.3: register named-range deps with the Graph's stripe
   509	        // index. The Graph's `register_range_dependency` populates BOTH
   510	        // the stripe map AND the `formula_to_range_deps` precision-check
   511	        // map atomically. We pass the formula's owning sheet as the
   512	        // fallback (today every conversion sets `range.sheet =
   513	        // Some(_)`, so the fallback is unused).
   514	        for (_, range) in &deps.named_ranges {
   515	            let range_ref = range_to_rangeref(*range);
   516	            self.graph
   517	                .register_range_dependency(formula_node, range_ref, formula_sheet);
   518	        }
   519	
   520	        if deps.is_volatile {
   521	            self.volatile_formulas.insert(formula_node);
   522	        }
   523	        for name in &deps.names {
   524	            self.name_to_formulas
   525	                .entry(Arc::clone(name))
   526	                .or_default()
   527	                .insert(formula_node);
   528	        }
   529	        if !deps.is_empty() || deps.is_volatile {
   530	            self.formula_deps.insert(formula_node, deps);
   531	        }
   532	    }
   533	
   534	    /// Internal: remove all session-side dep state for `formula_node`.
   535	    /// Used when a formula is re-bound (different text → different
   536	    /// deps) or cleared. Cleans `formula_deps`, the volatile set, the
   537	    /// name→formulas reverse index, and the cell→formulas reverse
   538	    /// index. Does NOT touch the `Graph`'s stripe state or edges —
   539	    /// those are revoked separately by the caller via
   540	    /// `Graph::clear_outgoing` + `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula`
   541	    /// (W5-50). **Both callers** (`extract_and_register_deps` for
   542	    /// the rebind path, `on_clear_formula` for the clear path) MUST
   543	    /// honor this contract — the clear-path miss was caught by the
   544	    /// W5-52 mega-audit (see commit history).
   545	    fn remove_formula_deps(&mut self, formula_node: NodeId) {
   546	        if let Some(prior) = self.formula_deps.remove(&formula_node) {
   547	            for name in &prior.names {
   548	                if let Some(set) = self.name_to_formulas.get_mut(name) {
   549	                    set.remove(&formula_node);
   550	                    if set.is_empty() {
   551	                        self.name_to_formulas.remove(name);
   552	                    }
   553	                }
   554	            }
   555	            for cell in &prior.cells {
   556	                if let Some(set) = self.cell_to_formulas.get_mut(cell) {
   557	                    set.remove(&formula_node);
   558	                    if set.is_empty() {
   559	                        self.cell_to_formulas.remove(cell);
   560	                    }
   561	                }
   562	            }
   563	        }
   564	        self.volatile_formulas.remove(&formula_node);
   565	    }
   566	
   567	    /// **G3-01 acceptance.** Deterministic rebuild from an existing
   568	    /// `Workbook`. Walks every formula cell in sorted `(sheet, row, col)`
   569	    /// order, adds a `CellNode` per cell, populates the cell index,
   570	    /// and (Phase 3.2) lex+parse+binds each formula to extract
   571	    /// dependencies.
   572	    ///
   573	    /// Determinism: same workbook → same node IDs in the same order.
   574	    /// Workbook's underlying `formula_cells` is a `HashMap` (arbitrary
   575	    /// iteration order), so we sort before adding nodes.
   576	    ///
   577	    /// Failures aggregate into [`RebuildResult::failures`] rather than
   578	    /// short-circuiting — the returned session is usable, just with
   579	    /// some formulas missing dep info. Matches Phase 2B.2's
   580	    /// `RecomputeResult` shape.
   581	    pub fn rebuild_from_workbook(wb: &Workbook) -> RebuildResult {
   582	        let mut session = Self::new();
   583	
   584	        // Snapshot + sort the formula list for determinism.
   585	        let mut formulas: Vec<(SheetId, RowId, ColId, Arc<str>)> = wb
   586	            .iter_formulas()
   587	            .map(|(s, r, c, f)| (s, r, c, Arc::clone(f)))
   588	            .collect();
   589	        formulas.sort_by_key(|a| (a.0, a.1, a.2));
   590	
   591	        let attempted = formulas.len();
   592	        let mut succeeded = 0;
   593	        let mut failures: Vec<RebuildFailure> = Vec::new();
   594	
   595	        // Phase 3.4: pre-pass — insert a CellNode for every formula
   596	        // before any extract runs. The Tarjan-side forward edges
   597	        // require `cell_index` to be COMPLETE at extract time so
   598	        // formula `=A1` at C1 can see A1's node and add the edge
   599	        // (otherwise late-arriving deps would be missed). Done in
   600	        // sorted order so node IDs stay deterministic.
   601	        for (sheet, row, col, _) in &formulas {
   602	            session.or_insert_cell_node(*sheet, *row, *col);
   603	        }
   604	
   605	        for (sheet, row, col, text) in formulas {
   606	            let node = session
   607	                .cell_node_for(sheet, row, col)
   608	                .expect("pre-pass inserted every formula's node");
   609	
   610	            // Phase 3.2: lex + parse + bind + extract deps. Failure
   611	            // accumulates; we keep going. Phase 3.3 passes the
   612	            // formula's owning sheet so the stripe register can use
   613	            // the correct fallback for any range with `sheet: None`.
   614	            match Self::bind_text(&text, sheet, wb) {
   615	                Ok(plan) => {
   616	                    session.extract_and_register_deps(node, sheet, &plan);
   617	                    succeeded += 1;
   618	                }
   619	                Err(error) => failures.push(RebuildFailure {
   620	                    sheet,
   760	    /// formulas referencing `name` (per `name_to_formulas`) dirty.
   761	    /// Today this reverse index is populated only by
   762	    /// `ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef` — see GAP-R-07 for the scalar-
   763	    /// NameRef precision gap (PlanCache `name_gen` still covers
   764	    /// correctness; per-name precision is the open follow-up).
   765	    ///
   766	    /// Phase 3.10 megaudit H2 (2026-05-12): the prior implementation
   767	    /// only marked DIRECT name-referencing formulas dirty, leaving
   768	    /// downstream-of-downstream stale. Fixed: each directly-affected
   769	    /// formula now seeds a `mark_dirty_from_cell_write` BFS at its
   770	    /// own cell address, fanning out the same way cell writes do.
   771	    pub fn on_set_name(&mut self, name: &str) {
   772	        self.hook_counts.set_name = self.hook_counts.set_name.saturating_add(1);
   773	        let upper = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   774	        let dependents: Vec<NodeId> = self
   775	            .name_to_formulas
   776	            .get(upper.as_str())
   777	            .map(|s| s.iter().copied().collect())
   778	            .unwrap_or_default();
   779	        for n in dependents {
   780	            self.dirty.insert(n);
   781	            // Phase 3.10 H2 fix: transitive fanout. Whatever depends on
   782	            // this name-referencing formula must also recompute when
   783	            // the name's target changes, just like for a direct cell
   784	            // edit. mark_dirty_from_cell_write does the BFS via the
   785	            // reverse-dep + stripe path so any chain or range dep is
   786	            // reached.
   787	            if let Some((s, r, c)) = self.cell_address_for(n) {
   788	                self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(s, r, c);
   789	            }
   790	        }
   791	    }
   792	
   793	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 5/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   794	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::add_sheet`. Phase 3.1 stub still: bumps
   795	    /// counter. No dirty effect — a new sheet contains no formulas
   796	    /// yet. Phase 4.6 (cross-sheet references) will use this to update
   797	    /// the graph's sheet-id tracking.
   798	    pub fn on_add_sheet(&mut self, _new_sheet: SheetId) {
   799	        self.hook_counts.add_sheet = self.hook_counts.add_sheet.saturating_add(1);
   800	    }
   801	
   802	    /// Phase 3.3 (single-hop) + Phase 3.4 (BFS): internal fanout —
   803	    /// for a write at `(sheet, row, col)`, mark every formula node
   804	    /// that TRANSITIVELY depends on this cell dirty. Two reverse-
   805	    /// direction lookups per visited node:
   806	    ///
   807	    /// 1. `Graph::dependents_for_cell` returns range-dep candidates,
   808	    ///    already filtered by the precision check (DIR-3-02).
   809	    /// 2. `cell_to_formulas[(s,r,c)]` returns direct-cell-dep
   810	    ///    formulas.
   811	    ///
   812	    /// Phase 3.4 added the BFS pass: for each newly-marked formula
   813	    /// `F`, look up `F`'s cell address and recurse. A chain
   814	    /// `=A1 → =B1=A1+1 → =C1=B1+1` thus marks both B1 AND C1 dirty
   815	    /// from a single edit at A1. The `dirty.insert(node)` check
   816	    /// returns false on duplicate insert → BFS visits each node at
   817	    /// most once → cycles in the dep graph are safe.
   818	    fn mark_dirty_from_cell_write(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   819	        use std::collections::VecDeque;
   820	        let mut queue: VecDeque<NodeId> = VecDeque::new();
   821	
   822	        // Phase 3.6 (AGG-3-02): invalidate any cached aggregate whose
   823	        // range contains this cell. Done BEFORE the dirty fanout so a
   824	        // formula whose recompute would otherwise hit a stale cache
   825	        // sees a fresh miss + recomputation. Precision is exact —
   826	        // writes outside every cached range are O(cache_size) with no
   827	        // entry removal.
   828	        self.aggregate_cache.invalidate_at(sheet, row, col);
   829	
   830	        // Seed with the direct fanout from the edited cell (which may
   831	        // not itself be a formula).
   832	        for dep in self.graph.dependents_for_cell(sheet, row, col) {
   833	            if self.dirty.insert(dep) {
   834	                queue.push_back(dep);
   835	            }
   836	        }
   837	        if let Some(set) = self.cell_to_formulas.get(&(sheet, row, col)) {
   838	            for &dep in set {
   839	                if self.dirty.insert(dep) {
   840	                    queue.push_back(dep);
   841	                }
   842	            }
   843	        }
   844	
   845	        // BFS: for every newly-marked formula, find what depends on
   846	        // ITS cell and propagate. This is the transitive closure of
   847	        // the reverse-dep relation, scoped to formula nodes.
   848	        while let Some(node) = queue.pop_front() {
   849	            let Some((ns, nr, nc)) = self.cell_address_for(node) else {
   850	                continue;
   851	            };
   852	            for dep in self.graph.dependents_for_cell(ns, nr, nc) {
   853	                if self.dirty.insert(dep) {
   854	                    queue.push_back(dep);
   855	                }
   856	            }
   857	            if let Some(set) = self.cell_to_formulas.get(&(ns, nr, nc)) {
   858	                let snapshot: Vec<NodeId> = set.iter().copied().collect();
   859	                for dep in snapshot {
   860	                    if self.dirty.insert(dep) {
   861	                        queue.push_back(dep);
   862	                    }
   863	                }
   864	            }
   865	        }
   866	    }
   867	
   868	    /// Phase 3.3: read-only view of the dirty set. Caller may iterate
   869	    /// to schedule recompute. Use `take_dirty` to atomically claim +
   870	    /// clear.
   871	    pub fn dirty_formulas(&self) -> &HashSet<NodeId> {
   872	        &self.dirty
   873	    }
   874	
   875	    /// Phase 3.3: is this formula currently queued for recompute?
   876	    pub fn is_dirty(&self, node: NodeId) -> bool {
   877	        self.dirty.contains(&node)
   878	    }
   879	
   880	    /// Phase 3.3: claim + clear the dirty set in one move. The Phase

 succeeded in 251ms:
     1	//! Topological scheduler — iterative Tarjan SCC over a dirty subset.
     2	//!
     3	//! Per CORR-23 (Week 3 Day 0 deep-read finding): both Formualizer (`engine/scheduler.rs:
     4	//! 88-239`) and HyperFormula (`TopSort.ts:18-92`) use iterative Tarjan SCC rather than
     5	//! Kahn's algorithm for topological scheduling. Reasons:
     6	//!
     7	//! 1. **Cycle detection in the same pass.** Tarjan partitions nodes into SCCs; SCCs of
     8	//!    size 1 with no self-loop are acyclic (go to `sorted`), everything else is cyclic
     9	//!    (goes to `cycled`). Kahn would need a separate "did all dirty nodes emit?" check
    10	//!    after the toposort to identify cycle membership.
    11	//!
    12	//! 2. **Direct API for the Phase 0 cycle policy.** The spec's Phase 0 minimum: cycled
    13	//!    cells become `CellError(Cycle)`; non-cycled cells evaluate in topological order.
    14	//!    The `(sorted, cycled)` partition is exactly what the evaluator wants.
    15	//!
    16	//! 3. **Iterative implementation handles deep graphs.** A 10K-deep dependency chain
    17	//!    would blow the default 8 MB stack with a recursive Tarjan. HyperFormula made the
    18	//!    same choice for JS call-stack reasons; ours is for arbitrary-depth Rust workbooks.
    19	//!
    20	//! 4. **Phase 0 minimum is non-iteration**: cycles are terminal errors, not states to
    21	//!    converge. Full iteration (with a `max_iterations` config) lands Phase 4+ when the
    22	//!    function library includes goal-seek / iterative-solve UDFs.
    23	//!
    24	//! ## Algorithm shape
    25	//!
    26	//! Standard iterative Tarjan with two stacks:
    27	//! - `dfs_stack: Vec<(NodeId, usize)>` — explicit DFS frame: (node, next-child-index).
    28	//!   Replaces the recursion stack of the textbook algorithm.
    29	//! - `scc_stack: Vec<NodeId>` — the Tarjan SCC stack of "currently-being-explored" nodes
    30	//!   that may belong to the same SCC as the current root.
    31	//!
    32	//! Per-node state in `FxHashMap`-equivalent (we use `std::HashMap` to avoid a new dep):
    33	//! - `indices[v]: u32` — DFS discovery order; assigned on first visit.
    34	//! - `lowlinks[v]: u32` — smallest index reachable from v's subtree.
    35	//! - `on_scc_stack[v]: bool` — is v currently on `scc_stack`?
    36	//!
    37	//! When a node's lowlink equals its index after all children are processed, it's the
    38	//! root of an SCC. We pop the SCC stack down to that node.
    39	//!
    40	//! ## Edge filtering
    41	//!
    42	//! The scheduler operates ON the dirty subset only. Edges to non-dirty nodes are skipped
    43	//! (those nodes aren't being re-evaluated; their current values are read as-is). A
    44	//! `dirty: HashSet<NodeId>` membership check filters `graph.outgoing(v)`.
    45	//!
    46	//! ## Output ordering
    47	//!
    48	//! Tarjan emits SCCs in **reverse topological order of the condensation**. For our
    49	//! graph orientation (edge `u → v` means "u depends on v"), this is exactly dependency-
    50	//! first order: when we collect `sorted` by appending SCCs as Tarjan finds them, the
    51	//! result is `[dependencies, ..., dependents]`. Callers evaluate `sorted[0]` first.
    52	
    53	use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
    54	
    55	use crate::graph::Graph;
    56	use crate::node::NodeId;
    57	
    58	/// The output of a scheduling pass.
    59	///
    60	/// `sorted` is in dependency-first order: evaluate `sorted[0]` first, then `sorted[1]`,
    61	/// etc. `cycled` is unordered — every cycled node should be assigned a cycle error.
    62	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
    63	pub struct Schedule {
    64	    pub sorted: Vec<NodeId>,
    65	    pub cycled: Vec<NodeId>,
    66	}
    67	
    68	impl Schedule {
    69	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
    70	        self.sorted.is_empty() && self.cycled.is_empty()
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    pub fn total_count(&self) -> usize {
    74	        self.sorted.len() + self.cycled.len()
    75	    }
    76	}
    77	
    78	/// Schedule the given dirty subset for evaluation. Returns `(sorted, cycled)` where
    79	/// `sorted` is in dependency-first topological order and `cycled` contains all nodes in
    80	/// non-trivial strongly connected components (size > 1) plus all self-loops (size 1 + an
    81	/// edge to self).
    82	///
    83	/// Edges to nodes outside `dirty_subset` are not traversed — those nodes aren't being
    84	/// re-evaluated, just read.
    85	///
    86	/// Duplicate entries in `dirty_subset` are tolerated (deduped internally via a HashSet).
    87	pub fn schedule(graph: &Graph, dirty_subset: &[NodeId]) -> Schedule {
    88	    let empty: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>> = HashMap::new();
    89	    schedule_with_supplemental(graph, dirty_subset, &empty)
    90	}
    91	
    92	/// **W5-50 — GAP-G-03 closure (small Option C).** Schedule with caller-
    93	/// provided supplemental adjacency layered on top of `graph.outgoing(v)`.
    94	/// Used by `CalcgraphSession::schedule_dirty` to inject range-induced
    95	/// edges that the Phase 0 graph itself doesn't carry: for each dirty
    96	/// formula `F` with range deps and a dirty formula `G` inside one of
    97	/// `F`'s ranges, the caller pushes a temp edge `F → G` into
    98	/// `supplemental[F]` so Tarjan can see the range-induced ordering /
    99	/// cycle.
   100	///
   101	/// The supplemental map is read-only during the scheduler call.
   102	/// Entries are appended to the corresponding `graph.outgoing(v)` slice
   103	/// — they don't replace real edges. Edges to non-dirty nodes are still
   104	/// filtered out (the existing dirty-subset contract holds).
   105	///
   106	/// Self-loops via supplemental ARE recognized as cycles (e.g.
   107	/// `A1 = SUM(A1:A1)` builds a supplemental `A1 → A1` edge; this single
   108	/// dirty formula gets emitted into `cycled`).
   109	///
   110	/// Backward compatibility: `schedule(graph, subset)` is now a thin
   111	/// wrapper that passes an empty supplemental map.
   112	pub fn schedule_with_supplemental(
   113	    graph: &Graph,
   114	    dirty_subset: &[NodeId],
   115	    supplemental: &HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>>,
   116	) -> Schedule {
   117	    let dirty: HashSet<NodeId> = dirty_subset.iter().copied().collect();
   118	    if dirty.is_empty() {
   119	        return Schedule::default();
   120	    }
   121	
   122	    let mut indices: HashMap<NodeId, u32> = HashMap::with_capacity(dirty.len());
   123	    let mut lowlinks: HashMap<NodeId, u32> = HashMap::with_capacity(dirty.len());
   124	    let mut on_scc_stack: HashMap<NodeId, bool> = HashMap::with_capacity(dirty.len());
   125	    let mut scc_stack: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::with_capacity(dirty.len());
   126	    let mut next_index: u32 = 0;
   127	
   128	    let mut sorted: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::with_capacity(dirty.len());
   129	    let mut cycled: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::new();
   130	
   131	    // Visit every node in the dirty subset. Iteration order is from the input slice (not
   132	    // the HashSet's randomized order) so callers get deterministic schedules from
   133	    // deterministic inputs. Deduplicated via `indices.contains_key`.
   134	    for &seed in dirty_subset {
   135	        if indices.contains_key(&seed) {
   136	            continue;
   137	        }
   138	        tarjan_dfs(
   139	            seed,
   140	            graph,
   141	            supplemental,
   142	            &dirty,
   143	            &mut indices,
   144	            &mut lowlinks,
   145	            &mut on_scc_stack,
   146	            &mut scc_stack,
   147	            &mut next_index,
   148	            &mut sorted,
   149	            &mut cycled,
   150	        );
   151	    }
   152	
   153	    Schedule { sorted, cycled }
   154	}
   155	
   156	#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
   157	fn tarjan_dfs(
   158	    seed: NodeId,
   159	    graph: &Graph,
   160	    supplemental: &HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>>,
   161	    dirty: &HashSet<NodeId>,
   162	    indices: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   163	    lowlinks: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   164	    on_scc_stack: &mut HashMap<NodeId, bool>,
   165	    scc_stack: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   166	    next_index: &mut u32,
   167	    sorted: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   168	    cycled: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   169	) {
   170	    // Explicit DFS frame: (node, child index in outgoing(node) — within-dirty filter).
   171	    // We compute the child slice once at first visit + iterate it index-by-index across
   172	    // frames. Filtering "is this child in dirty?" happens at child-visit time so we don't
   173	    // pre-allocate filtered child vectors per node (the hot path under no-cycles is one
   174	    // outgoing-slice scan per node, period).
   175	    //
   176	    // W5-50: the "child slice" is now `graph.outgoing(v)` concatenated with
   177	    // `supplemental.get(&v)` (if any). Indices < outgoing.len() refer to the real graph;
   178	    // indices >= outgoing.len() refer to the supplemental list. Constant-time per child.
   179	    let mut dfs_stack: Vec<(NodeId, usize)> = Vec::new();
   180	
   181	    // First visit of seed.
   182	    visit_first(seed, next_index, indices, lowlinks, on_scc_stack, scc_stack);
   183	    dfs_stack.push((seed, 0));
   184	
   185	    while let Some(&(v, child_idx)) = dfs_stack.last() {
   186	        let outgoing = graph.outgoing(v);
   187	        let supp: &[NodeId] = supplemental.get(&v).map(|v| v.as_slice()).unwrap_or(&[]);
   188	        let total_children = outgoing.len() + supp.len();
   189	
   190	        // Find the next not-yet-considered child that's in the dirty subset.
   191	        let mut next_child: Option<NodeId> = None;
   192	        let mut new_child_idx = child_idx;
   193	        while new_child_idx < total_children {
   194	            let candidate = if new_child_idx < outgoing.len() {
   195	                outgoing[new_child_idx]
   196	            } else {
   197	                supp[new_child_idx - outgoing.len()]
   198	            };
   199	            new_child_idx += 1;
   200	            if dirty.contains(&candidate) {
   201	                next_child = Some(candidate);
   202	                break;
   203	            }
   204	        }
   205	
   206	        // Update this frame's child cursor regardless of whether we recurse or finish.
   207	        let frame_idx = dfs_stack.len() - 1;
   208	        dfs_stack[frame_idx].1 = new_child_idx;
   209	
   210	        if let Some(w) = next_child {
   211	            if !indices.contains_key(&w) {
   212	                // Unvisited dirty child — recurse.
   213	                visit_first(w, next_index, indices, lowlinks, on_scc_stack, scc_stack);
   214	                dfs_stack.push((w, 0));
   215	            } else if *on_scc_stack
   216	                .get(&w)
   217	                .expect("topo invariant: every key in `indices` must also be in `on_scc_stack` — visit_first populates both atomically")
   218	            {
   219	                // Phase 2A.7 audit M13: switched from `unwrap_or(&false)` to `.expect(...)`.
   220	                // The prior fallback silently treated "missing key" as "not on stack",
   221	                // conflating "not yet visited" with "explicitly off-stack" — masking a
   222	                // Tarjan invariant violation. Per no-fallbacks rule, an invariant
   223	                // violation must panic loudly, not produce subtly-wrong SCC results.
   224	                //
   225	                // Back-edge or cross-edge to a node currently on the SCC stack — update
   226	                // v's lowlink with w's index.
   227	                let w_idx = indices[&w];
   228	                let v_low = lowlinks[&v];
   229	                if w_idx < v_low {
   230	                    lowlinks.insert(v, w_idx);
   231	                }
   232	            }
   233	            // Else: w is in a completed SCC; ignore the cross-edge.
   234	        } else {
   235	            // All children processed — post-order completion.
   236	            dfs_stack.pop();
   237	
   238	            // If v is the root of an SCC, pop it off the SCC stack.
   239	            if lowlinks[&v] == indices[&v] {
   240	                let mut scc: Vec<NodeId> = Vec::new();
   241	                loop {
   242	                    let w = scc_stack
   243	                        .pop()
   244	                        .expect("scc_stack drained mid-pop — algorithm invariant violated");
   245	                    on_scc_stack.insert(w, false);
   246	                    scc.push(w);
   247	                    if w == v {
   248	                        break;
   249	                    }
   250	                }
   251	
   252	                // Classify the SCC. Self-loops can come from the real graph
   253	                // OR the supplemental map — both count as cycles.
   254	                let is_cycle = scc.len() > 1
   255	                    || (scc.len() == 1 && {
   256	                        let self_id = scc[0];
   257	                        let real_self_loop = graph
   258	                            .outgoing(self_id)
   259	                            .iter()
   260	                            .any(|&t| t == self_id && dirty.contains(&t));

exec

 succeeded in 224ms:
   252	                // Classify the SCC. Self-loops can come from the real graph
   253	                // OR the supplemental map — both count as cycles.
   254	                let is_cycle = scc.len() > 1
   255	                    || (scc.len() == 1 && {
   256	                        let self_id = scc[0];
   257	                        let real_self_loop = graph
   258	                            .outgoing(self_id)
   259	                            .iter()
   260	                            .any(|&t| t == self_id && dirty.contains(&t));
   261	                        let supp_self_loop = supplemental
   262	                            .get(&self_id)
   263	                            .map(|s| s.iter().any(|&t| t == self_id && dirty.contains(&t)))
   264	                            .unwrap_or(false);
   265	                        real_self_loop || supp_self_loop
   266	                    });
   267	                if is_cycle {
   268	                    cycled.extend(scc);
   269	                } else {
   270	                    // Single acyclic node — append to sorted.
   271	                    sorted.extend(scc);
   272	                }
   273	            }
   274	
   275	            // Propagate v's lowlink up to its parent (post-order).
   276	            if let Some(&(parent, _)) = dfs_stack.last() {
   277	                let v_low = lowlinks[&v];
   278	                let p_low = lowlinks[&parent];
   279	                if v_low < p_low {
   280	                    lowlinks.insert(parent, v_low);
   281	                }
   282	            }
   283	        }
   284	    }
   285	}
   286	
   287	fn visit_first(
   288	    v: NodeId,
   289	    next_index: &mut u32,
   290	    indices: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   291	    lowlinks: &mut HashMap<NodeId, u32>,
   292	    on_scc_stack: &mut HashMap<NodeId, bool>,
   293	    scc_stack: &mut Vec<NodeId>,
   294	) {
   295	    let idx = *next_index;
   296	    *next_index = next_index
   297	        .checked_add(1)
   298	        .expect("Tarjan next_index overflow u32 — graphs > 4.3B nodes are not supported");
   299	    indices.insert(v, idx);
   300	    lowlinks.insert(v, idx);
   301	    scc_stack.push(v);
   302	    on_scc_stack.insert(v, true);
   303	}

   880	    /// Phase 3.3: claim + clear the dirty set in one move. The Phase
   881	    /// 3.4 Tarjan SCC scheduler will call this once per recompute
   882	    /// cycle. Returning `HashSet<NodeId>` rather than a slice lets
   883	    /// callers re-sort / topologically order without re-allocating.
   884	    pub fn take_dirty(&mut self) -> HashSet<NodeId> {
   885	        std::mem::take(&mut self.dirty)
   886	    }
   887	
   888	    /// **Phase 3.7 (2026-05-12) — VOL-3-01/02 entry point.** Mark every
   889	    /// volatile formula (NOW / RAND / TODAY / RANDBETWEEN / RANDARRAY
   890	    /// / INDIRECT / OFFSET / INFO / CELL, per `is_volatile_function`)
   891	    /// dirty AND fan out the transitive reverse-dep graph from each
   892	    /// volatile cell so downstream formulas recompute too. This is
   893	    /// the "F9 / explicit recalc" trigger.
   894	    ///
   895	    /// Returns the number of volatile formulas marked. Zero means no
   896	    /// volatile formulas exist in the workbook — `recompute_dirty`
   897	    /// after this call will be a no-op.
   898	    pub fn mark_volatile_dirty(&mut self) -> usize {
   899	        let volatile: Vec<NodeId> = self.volatile_formulas.iter().copied().collect();
   900	        let count = volatile.len();
   901	        for v in volatile {
   902	            self.dirty.insert(v);
   903	            // Fan out from the volatile cell's address so downstream
   904	            // formulas reading its value also recompute (VOL-3-02).
   905	            // `mark_dirty_from_cell_write` does BFS via the reverse-
   906	            // dep + stripe path, so any chain or range dep is reached.
   907	            if let Some((s, r, c)) = self.cell_address_for(v) {
   908	                self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(s, r, c);
   909	            }
   910	        }
   911	        count
   912	    }

   947	    /// Phase 3.4: address lookup for a formula node. The graph's
   948	    /// `Node::Cell` payload carries `(sheet, row, col)` — this is the
   949	    /// inverse of `cell_node_for`. Returns `None` if the node is not
   950	    /// a Cell variant (today every NodeId in `cell_index` is a Cell;
   951	    /// Phase 4 may add Range/Region nodes that aren't formula cells).
   952	    pub fn cell_address_for(&self, node: NodeId) -> Option<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)> {
   953	        match self.graph.node(node) {
   954	            Node::Cell(CellNode { sheet, row, col }) => Some((*sheet, *row, *col)),
   955	            _ => None,
   956	        }
   957	    }

   959	    /// **Phase 3.4 acceptance (SCH-3-01..04 entry point).** Atomically
   960	    /// claim the dirty set and run the Phase 0 W3-3 iterative Tarjan
   961	    /// scheduler over it. The returned [`Schedule`] partitions nodes:
   962	    ///
   963	    /// - `sorted`: dependency-first topological order. Evaluate
   964	    ///   `sorted[0]` before `sorted[1]`, etc.
   965	    /// - `cycled`: nodes in a non-trivial strongly connected component
   966	    ///   (size > 1) or with a self-loop. Per Phase 0 spec these get
   967	    ///   `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Circ)`.
   968	    ///
   969	    /// The dirty set is cleared by this call. Edges to non-dirty
   970	    /// nodes are not traversed (those nodes aren't being recomputed;
   971	    /// their current values are read as-is). Input ordering is
   972	    /// sorted-by-NodeId before the scheduler runs so SCH-3-03
   973	    /// determinism holds: same workbook + same edits → same schedule
   974	    /// across runs (the underlying scheduler is already deterministic
   975	    /// in its input slice order; we just normalize HashSet iteration).
   976	    pub fn schedule_dirty(&mut self) -> Schedule {
   977	        let mut dirty_vec: Vec<NodeId> = self.dirty.drain().collect();
   978	        dirty_vec.sort();
   979	        // W5-50 (GAP-G-03 closure): inject supplemental adjacency so
   980	        // Tarjan can see range-induced ordering / cycles. The Phase 0
