   976	    pub fn schedule_dirty(&mut self) -> Schedule {
   977	        let mut dirty_vec: Vec<NodeId> = self.dirty.drain().collect();
   978	        dirty_vec.sort();
   979	        // W5-50 (GAP-G-03 closure): inject supplemental adjacency so
   980	        // Tarjan can see range-induced ordering / cycles. The Phase 0
   981	        // graph stores range deps in stripes + `formula_to_range_deps`
   982	        // rather than as `add_edge` edges (the A4 acceptance: SUM(A:A)
   983	        // must compress to ONE RangeRef, not 25M edges). For Tarjan we
   984	        // expand range deps lazily into temp edges F → G where G is a
   985	        // dirty formula inside one of F's ranges.
   986	        let supplemental = self.build_range_supplemental(&dirty_vec);
   987	        schedule_with_supplemental(&self.graph, &dirty_vec, &supplemental)
   988	    }
   989	
   990	    /// W5-50 — build the supplemental adjacency for
   991	    /// `topo::schedule_with_supplemental`. For each dirty formula `F`
   992	    /// with at least one range dep, and each OTHER (or same — for
   993	    /// self-range cycles) dirty formula `G` whose cell address falls
   994	    /// inside one of F's ranges, emit a temp edge `F → G`.
   995	    ///
   996	    /// Per Codex (W5-49 review): scope is **dirty formula nodes only**.
   997	    /// Non-formula cells (literals) inside the range are read as
   998	    /// current values during recompute, NOT scheduled — adding edges
   999	    /// to them would just produce no-op Tarjan filtering.
  1000	    ///
  1001	    /// Determinism: `dirty_vec` is sorted by NodeId before this call
  1002	    /// runs, so the supplemental Vec entries land in a deterministic
  1003	    /// order (same dirty set + same graph → same supplemental → same
  1004	    /// Schedule).
  1005	    ///
  1006	    /// Complexity: O(|dirty_vec|² × avg_ranges_per_formula). Typical
  1007	    /// edits have small dirty sets; the inner loop is a tight
  1008	    /// `range_contains_rowcol` check. If profiling later shows pain,
  1009	    /// switching to per-range bucketing (build a per-stripe index of
  1010	    /// dirty formulas, intersect with range bounds) is the path.
  1011	    fn build_range_supplemental(&self, dirty_vec: &[NodeId]) -> HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>> {
  1012	        // Resolve cell addresses for every dirty FORMULA node. Non-
  1013	        // formula NodeIds (none exist today — the graph only adds Cell
  1014	        // nodes for formula cells) filter out via cell_address_for
  1015	        // returning None.
  1016	        let dirty_addrs: Vec<(NodeId, SheetId, RowId, ColId)> = dirty_vec
  1017	            .iter()
  1018	            .filter_map(|&n| self.cell_address_for(n).map(|(s, r, c)| (n, s, r, c)))
  1019	            .collect();
  1020	
  1021	        let mut supplemental: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>> = HashMap::new();
  1022	        for &(f, fs, _, _) in &dirty_addrs {
  1023	            let ranges = self.graph.range_deps_for(f);
  1024	            if ranges.is_empty() {
  1025	                continue;
  1026	            }
  1027	            // W5-52 (audit closure, Sonnet MEDIUM): deduplicate G across
  1028	            // F's multiple ranges. Without this, a formula with two
  1029	            // overlapping ranges (e.g., SUM(A1:A10) + SUM(A1:A5)) that
  1030	            // both contain the same dirty G would push G twice into
  1031	            // `supplemental[F]`. Tarjan handles duplicate edges
  1032	            // correctly, but the Vec growth is wasted work — and this
  1033	            // gets worse as Phase 4.3 V2 adds multi-range functions.
  1034	            // `added.insert(g)` returns `true` on first add only.
  1035	            let mut added: HashSet<NodeId> = HashSet::new();
  1036	            for range in ranges {
  1037	                let resolved_sheet = Graph::range_ref_sheet(range).unwrap_or(fs);
  1038	                for &(g, gs, gr, gc) in &dirty_addrs {
  1039	                    if gs != resolved_sheet {
  1040	                        continue;
  1041	                    }
  1042	                    if range_contains_rowcol(range, gr, gc) && added.insert(g) {
  1043	                        supplemental.entry(f).or_default().push(g);
  1044	                    }
  1045	                }
  1046	            }
  1047	        }
  1048	        supplemental
  1049	    }
  1050	}

    53	## Architectural decisions made this session
    54	
    55	### Decision A: GAP-G-01 + GAP-G-03 (W5-49 → W5-50, audit-closed W5-52)
    56	
    57	**Problem:** Phase 0 `Graph` was append-only. Rebinding a formula left stale forward edges → false `#CIRC!` cycles (megaudit H3, GAP-G-01). Range deps were stored in stripes + `formula_to_range_deps` but never `add_edge`'d, so Tarjan couldn't see range-induced ordering or cycles (megaudit H1, GAP-G-03).
    58	
    59	**Decision:** Option A (per-formula revocation API: `Graph::clear_outgoing` + `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula` + `StripeIndex::clear_for_formula` backed by `formula_to_stripe_keys` reverse index) + small Option C (`topo::schedule_with_supplemental` injecting temp `F→G` edges at recompute time for dirty formula pairs inside ranges). FormulaRegion binder (Option B) deferred to Phase 4.7.
    60	
    61	**Codex-reviewed:** Yes (full Codex review at `docs/audits/2026-05-13-codex-graph-decision-review.txt`).
    62	
    63	**Status:** Shipped W5-50 (rebind path) + W5-52 (clear-formula path follow-up after Codex+Sonnet mega-audit found the missed wiring). Both `extract_and_register_deps` and `on_clear_formula` now revoke graph state.
    64	
    65	**Decision doc:** `docs/architecture/2026-05-13-graph-storage-decision.md`.
    66	
    67	### Decision B: GAP-F-05 — RangeAwareFn parallel function table (W5-53)
    68	
    69	**Problem:** `ScalarFn = fn(&[Value]) -> Value` couldn't carry per-argument range-vs-scalar metadata. SUMIF/COUNTIF/VLOOKUP/etc. need to know "this arg is a range, that arg is criteria."
    70	
    71	**Decision:** Parallel `RangeAwareFn = fn(&[FnArg]) -> Value` table in `FunctionRegistry`. `enum FnArg { Scalar(Value), Range { values, rows, cols } }`. Eval dispatch checks `lookup_range_aware` FIRST, constructs `Vec<FnArg>` from `ExprPlan` args (AggregateNameRef → Range via `env.read_range_with_shape`, others → Scalar via recursive eval). The 60+ existing scalar functions stay under `ScalarFn` unchanged. NO migration.
    72	
    73	**Status:** Shipped W5-53 (infra + SUMIF + COUNTIF). Refactored W5-54 to make `FnArg::Range` a struct variant `{ values, rows, cols }` for 2D-shape-aware lookups (VLOOKUP/HLOOKUP/INDEX).
    74	
    75	**Filed gap doc:** `docs/known-gaps.md` GAP-F-05 (now closed).
    76	
    77	### Decision C: FnArg::Range shape contract (W5-54)
    78	
    79	**Problem:** VLOOKUP / HLOOKUP / INDEX need to address by `(row, col)` from a 2D range. Initial W5-53 `FnArg::Range(Vec<Value>)` flat-iteration lost shape.
    80

     1	# Audit protocol — Quantbook engine

     3	**Status:** MANDATORY for every Claude Code window working on the Quantbook engine.

    12

The biggest risk is silent doc/code divergence becoming the next window's starting premise — that already almost happened in W5-50 (`on_clear_formula` clear-path bug shipped under "GAP-G-01 closed" framing; caught only by the W5-52 parallel mega-audit). This protocol prevents that.

    18

    21	- **The W5-49 → W5-58 session ran past this limit** under explicit user direction. The user is responsible for that override — DO NOT do it on your own.

    26

    28

    45	
    46	# 4. build-flags guard (catches -Ctarget-cpu=native sneaking in)

    73	## Per-phase mega-audit (MANDATORY — the W5-52 pattern)
    74	
    75	After every phase completion OR substantial run (e.g. ≥3 commits in one feature area), dispatch **TWO independent auditors in parallel**: Codex and a Sonnet agent. Why both: the W5-52 audit found a HIGH bug because both auditors independently flagged it — single-auditor review would not have given the same confidence.
    76	
    77	### Auditor brief template
    78	
    79	The brief MUST include:
    80	1. **Context** — branch state, HEAD, commits to audit (shas), test count, gate status
    81	2. **Required reading** — exact file paths (docs + code) the auditor must read
    82	3. **Session ledger** — commit-by-commit summary
    83	4. **Specific concerns** — what to challenge (don't just ask "is it correct"; ask 6-8 concrete questions, like the W5-49 review brief did)
    84	5. **What to report** — HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW + test gaps + verdict
    85	
    86	Save brief at `docs/audits/<date>-<scope>-megaudit-prompt.md`.
    87	
    88	### Codex dispatch
    89	
    90	```bash
    91	mac zsh -lc 'codex exec \
    92	  --skip-git-repo-check \
    93	  --sandbox read-only \
    94	  --color never \
    95	  -C /Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook \
    96	  < /Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/audits/<date>-<scope>-megaudit-prompt.md \
    97	  > /Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/audits/<date>-<scope>-megaudit-codex.txt \
    98	  2>&1; echo CODEX_EXIT=$?'
    99	```
   100	
   101	Run **in background** (`run_in_background: true` on Bash tool, 600000ms timeout).
   102	
   103	### Sonnet agent dispatch
   104	
   105	Use the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: "general-purpose"` and `model: "sonnet"`. Pass the same brief. Tell it to save its report at `docs/audits/<date>-<scope>-megaudit-sonnet.md`. Run **in background** as well so both auditors execute in parallel.
   106	
   107	### Synthesis (Codex Patience rule)
   108	
   109	- **MANDATORY:** wait for BOTH to complete before reading either. Do not pre-write conclusions.
   110	- **MANDATORY:** read Codex's full output (often 5-10k lines). Critical findings are often near the end.
   111	- Compare findings:
   112	  - **HIGH found by BOTH** = real correctness bug. Fix immediately before any other work.
   113	  - **HIGH found by ONE** = investigate carefully; usually real but verify with code reading.
   114	  - **MEDIUM** = either fix or file in `known-gaps.md` with target phase.
   115	  - **LOW / doc-drift** = fix immediately if 5-min; else defer with explicit note.
   116	
   117	### Closure commit
   118	
   119	After fixes, commit a closure commit (e.g. `W5-NN / <scope>-megaudit-closure: <HIGH fix subjects>`). Reference both auditors in the commit message.
   120	
   260	
   261	### 8. `FunctionRegistry::names()` only returns scalar names
   262	
   263	The `names()` iterator on `FunctionRegistry` only walks the scalar `fns` table — it does NOT include range-aware names. Any code that enumerates the registry (e.g. to dump the function list) must walk both `fns` AND `range_aware_fns`. Currently no caller does this; this is a latent bug if any enumeration code is added.
   264	
   265	### 9. `Graph::clear_outgoing` / `clear_range_deps_for_formula` bump revision even on no-op
   266	
   267	Both APIs always bump `Graph::revision()` even when called on a node with no outgoing edges / no range deps. State-idempotent, revision-not-idempotent. Affects only callers using revision as a cheapness proxy — none today, but Phase 4.7 array formulas may add one.
   268	
   269	### 10. VEQ + supplemental edges — UNTESTED interaction
   270	
   271	Codex's W5-49 watch list named: "VEQ behavior for range-dep formulas (Phase 3.8 short-circuit + supplemental edges must compose)." Phase 3.8's value-equality short-circuit may interact with W5-50's `supplemental` edges in unexpected ways. No test covers this composition. Add a regression test if Phase 4.7 (or earlier) touches either path, OR if a real bug surfaces in the field.
   272	
   273	### 11. CEILING(n, 0) vs FLOOR(n, 0) divergence
   274	
   275	Excel canon (NOT a bug):
   276	- `CEILING(n, 0)` → returns `0` even for non-zero `n`.
   277	- `FLOOR(n, 0)` → returns `#DIV/0!` for non-zero `n`; `0` for `n=0`.
   278	
   279	Easy to "normalize" these two into matching behavior. DON'T — Excel disagrees with itself on the two functions.
   280	
   281	### 12. macOS `/tmp/xcrun_db` warnings under sandbox
   282	
   283	When Codex runs with `--sandbox read-only`, git emits:
   284	```
   285	warning: confstr() failed with code 5: couldn't get path of DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR; using /tmp instead
   286	```
   287	
   288	These are NOT failures. Ignore.
   289	
   290	### 13. 24+ commits unpushed BY DESIGN
   291	
   292	The session intentionally accumulates commits locally per CLAUDE.md's "never push unless asked" rule. The next window should default to NOT pushing. Only push when the user explicitly says so.
   293	
   294	### 14. Repo root broad search
   295	
   296	The workspace root has `node_modules/`, session-export `.txt` files, etc. Broad `rg` / `find` from repo root returns huge unrelated results. Scope to `quantbook-engine/` unless intentionally checking workspace-level files.
   297	
   298	### 15. Pre-commit husky hook
   299	
   300	`git commit` triggers `npm run -s precommit` (a husky hook). It's automatic; no special action needed unless it fails. Per CLAUDE.md, DO NOT bypass with `--no-verify` — fix the underlying issue.
   301	
   302	---
   303	
   304	## Audit-closure expectations
   305	
   306	After any audit (per-phase mega-audit, or per-commit gate failure that surfaces a finding):
   307	
   308	- **HIGH (correctness bug, false-confidence ship):** fix IMMEDIATELY. Write a closure commit (`W5-NN / <scope>-audit-closure`). Document fix + reference auditor finding.
   309	- **MEDIUM (overstatement, scope gap, missing test):** fix if 5-min; otherwise file in `docs/known-gaps.md` with target phase. NEVER ignore.
   310	- **LOW (doc typo, naming inconsistency):** fix immediately during closure; or batch into the next regular commit with an "audit cleanup" subject line.
   311	
   312	**FORBIDDEN:**
   313	- Ignoring a HIGH finding.
   314	- Calling something "fixed" without a regression test.
   315	- Claiming a phase complete with open MEDIUM findings unfiled.
   316	
   317	---
   318	
   319	## When NOT to push
   320	
   321	**MANDATORY default: don't push.** Per CLAUDE.md and the session's accumulated 24+ local commits, push only when:
   322	
   323	- The user explicitly says "push", or
   324	- A planned CI run / collaborator handoff requires it (with explicit user approval).
   325	
   326	`git push` is a hard-to-reverse, externally-visible action; treat it accordingly.

   331	
   332	Track cycles explicitly in your `TaskCreate` / `TaskList` workflow:
   333	- Cycle 1: design / decision / Plan mode work
   334	- Cycle 2: implementation OR audit-closure
   335	- If contemplating cycle 3: write a handoff, end the session.

   340

   342	User request comes in
   343	  │
   344	  ├─ Trivial 1-line fix? → Direct edit + 7 gates + commit
   345	  │
   346	  ├─ Non-trivial implementation?
   347	  │   ├─ Architectural choice (multiple valid approaches)?
   348	  │   │   → Plan mode + Codex BEFORE implementation + decision doc
   349	  │   ├─ New batch of similar functions (e.g. text wave 2)?
   350	  │   │   → Implement with 7 gates per commit + per-batch audit
   351	  │   └─ Substantial cross-cutting change?
   352	  │       → Plan mode + Codex review + multi-cycle plan
   353	  │
   354	  ├─ Phase boundary or end of substantial run?
   355	  │   → MANDATORY Codex + Sonnet parallel mega-audit
   356	  │
   357	  └─ Hit cycle 3?
   358	      → STOP. Write handoff. End session.
   359	```

    52	## PART I - Strategic Posture
    53	
    54	### 1. How We Got Here
    55	
    56	Phase 0 proved that the engine shape is viable. The locked architectural gates are still binding:
    57	
    58	- T1-D02: hand-rolled dependency graph on the hot path, not petgraph.
    59	- T1-D03: direct Arrow kernels on the hot path, not Polars or DataFusion.
    60	- T1-D04: pulp plus multiversion SIMD.
    61	- T1-D05: Loro reserved for operation history and collaboration substrate work, not evaluator state.
    62	- T1-D06: stock VS Code APIs for v1.
    63	- T1-D07: Tier-0 wedge is Monaco-powered formula bar plus Python UDFs.
    64	- T2-D01: `.qbook/` directory plus `workbook.toml` envelope.
    65	- T3-D03: roughly 260 v1 spreadsheet functions.
    66	
    67	Phase 1 built the first live formula path: lexer, parser, AST printer, scalar binding, scalar evaluation, `.qbook/` persistence, `WorkbookRuntime::set_formula`, and `recompute_all`. It made the engine useful, but it did not make it architecturally complete. `recompute_all` still walks formula cells in map order, so dependency chains can compute incorrectly.
    68	
    69	Phase 2A closed a large correctness and persistence pass: defined names, transactions, load-and-recompute, dotted function identifiers, schema v2, crash-safe save, Excel-canon coercion fixes, deterministic formula fingerprints, error ergonomics, and audit closure. Phase 2A.3 then shipped Loro-backed op-log scaffolding: `ql-oplog`, typed `Op` variants, Loro snapshot persistence, replay, runtime and transaction producer wiring, and `.qbook/oplog.bin` persistence. The stale `docs/phase2/entry-plan.md` and `docs/phase2/exit-packet.md` still say 2A.3 is deferred; this plan supersedes that statement and requires a doc-rot repair pass in Phase 2B.
    70	
    71	The engine now has 816 workspace tests and 7 green gates. That is a floor, not a trophy. The major unresolved problem is that the "fast engine" (`ql-calcgraph`, SIMD region lowering, storage profiles) and the "runtime engine" (`WorkbookRuntime`, per-formula scalar path) still do not form one engine.
    72	
    73	Reference reading already changed the plan:
    74	
    75	- `docs/phase0/references-reading-log.md` corrected the stripe design to Formualizer-style per-row/per-column/block stripes (`CORR-21`).
    76	- Range aggregate caching is a Phase 3/4 feature informed by HyperFormula, not Formualizer (`CORR-22`).
    77	- Scheduling should use Tarjan SCC on dirty subsets (`CORR-23`).
    78	- Graph tests should expose typed accessors, not serialized graph dumps (`CORR-24`).
    79	- Storage eventually needs user and computed overlays (`CORR-25`).
    80

    72	| GAP-F-04 | No NIST `numacc4` extreme test fixture for Welford stats | `ql-functions/src/welford.rs` covers numacc3 only | Engine | Post-v1 stats hardening |
    73	| ~~GAP-F-05~~ | ~~`ScalarFn` cannot carry range-vs-scalar argument metadata.~~ **CLOSED in Engine Phase 4.3 V2 (W5-53).** Approach (a) — parallel `RangeAwareFn = fn(&[FnArg]) -> Value` table — chosen instead of full migration of the ~60 existing scalar entries. `FnArg { Scalar(Value), Range(Vec<Value>) }` lives in `crates/ql-functions/src/range_aware_fns.rs`. `FunctionRegistry` carries a second table `range_aware_fns: HashMap<&'static str, RangeAwareFn>` with `register_range_aware` / `lookup_range_aware`. Cross-table name collision panics at registration. Eval dispatch in `crates/ql-exec/src/scalar.rs::eval_scalar_with_cache` checks `lookup_range_aware` FIRST and constructs `Vec<FnArg>` from `ExprPlan` args (AggregateNameRef → Range via env.read_range, other plans → Scalar via recursive eval). SUMIF + COUNTIF shipped on this infra in the same commit; SUMIFS / AVERAGEIF / SUMPRODUCT / VLOOKUP / HLOOKUP / MATCH / INDEX / CHOOSE are V2 follow-ups now safely unblocked. 5 e2e tests in `workbook_runtime` + 25 unit tests in `range_fns` + 4 registry conflict/disjoint tests + extended `is_aggregate_function_lists_only_registered_aggregates` invariant test. |

   158	
   159	## How to use this doc
   160	
   161	- **Adding a gap:** new row in the relevant section. Always include reproduce path + target Engine phase.
   162	- **Closing a gap:** move the row to `Closed gaps` with closing commit SHA + date.
   163	- **At every phase exit:** sweep this list, move closed items, add any new ones surfaced during the phase.
   164	- **No gap stays untargeted.** If a gap can't fit any planned phase, that's a planning problem — escalate to MASTER-PLAN.md edit.

  2531	            "VAR.P", "STDEV", "STDEV.S", "STDEV.P",
  2532	        ] {
  2533	            assert!(
  2534	                reg.lookup(name).is_some(),
  2535	                "is_aggregate_function lists {name:?} but it's not in default_registry"
  2536	            );
  2537	        }

  2539	        // aware names are also in is_aggregate_function but live in
  2540	        // the parallel range_aware_fns table; check via
  2541	        // lookup_range_aware.

  2543	            "SUMIF",
  2544	            "COUNTIF",
  2545	            "MATCH",
  2546	            "INDEX",
  2547	            "VLOOKUP",

  2561	            "MODE.SNGL",

  2565	                "is_aggregate_function lists {name:?} (range-aware variant) but \

  2567	            );

  2569	                reg.lookup(name).is_none(),
  2570	                "{name:?} is range-aware ONLY; must not also appear in the scalar table"
  2571	            );
  2572	        }

  2574	        // is_aggregate_function does NOT claim it. We can't directly call
  2575	        // is_aggregate_function (private), but we can verify via behavior:
  3935	        assert!(

  3961	    /// SUMIF over a named range with numeric criteria.
  3962	    /// `Sales = A1:A5 = [1, 5, 5, 10, 5]`. `=SUMIF(Sales, 5)` should
  3963	    /// match the three 5's → 15.
  3964	    #[test]
  3965	    fn w5_53_sumif_named_range_numeric_criteria() {
  3966	        use ql_storage::NamedTarget;
  3967	        use ql_types::Range;
  3968	        let mut wb = make_runtime_workbook();
  3969	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 0, Value::Number(1.0));
  3970	        wb.put_at(0, 1, 0, Value::Number(5.0));
  3971	        wb.put_at(0, 2, 0, Value::Number(5.0));
  3972	        wb.put_at(0, 3, 0, Value::Number(10.0));
  3973	        wb.put_at(0, 4, 0, Value::Number(5.0));
  3974	        wb.set_name("Sales", NamedTarget::Range(Range::new(0, 0, 0, 4, 0)))
  3975	            .unwrap();
  3976	        let reg = default_registry();
  3977	        let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::new(&mut wb, &reg);
  3978	        let v = rt.set_formula(0, 0, 1, "SUMIF(Sales, 5)").unwrap();
  3979	        assert_eq!(v, Value::Number(15.0));
  3980	    }

  4054	    /// SUMIF interacts correctly with the recompute path (Phase 3.6
  4055	    /// aggregate cache is BYPASSED for range-aware functions — they
  4056	    /// are NOT in `is_aggregate_function`'s set, so they recompute
  4057	    /// on every edit). When a cell in the criteria range changes,
  4058	    /// the SUMIF formula must re-evaluate.
  4059	    #[test]
  4060	    fn w5_53_sumif_recomputes_on_range_cell_change() {
  4061	        use ql_storage::NamedTarget;
  4062	        use ql_types::Range;
  4063	        let mut wb = make_runtime_workbook();
  4064	        for r in 0..5 {
  4065	            wb.put_at(0, r, 0, Value::Number(r as f64 + 1.0));
  4066	        }
  4067	        wb.set_name("Vals", NamedTarget::Range(Range::new(0, 0, 0, 4, 0)))
  4068	            .unwrap();
  4069	        let reg = default_registry();
  4070	        let mut graph = crate::CalcgraphSession::new();
  4071	        {
  4072	            let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::with_graph(&mut wb, &reg, &mut graph);
  4073	            let v = rt.set_formula(0, 0, 1, "SUMIF(Vals, \">2\")").unwrap();
  4074	            assert_eq!(v, Value::Number(3.0 + 4.0 + 5.0));
  4075	        }
  4076	        // Edit a cell in Vals — bumps A1 from 1.0 to 100.0.
  4077	        {
  4078	            let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::with_graph(&mut wb, &reg, &mut graph);
  4079	            rt.set_value(0, 0, 0, Value::Number(100.0)).unwrap();
  4080	            let _ = rt.recompute_dirty().expect("graph attached");
  4081	        }
  4082	        // SUMIF formula at B1 should now include 100 → 100+3+4+5 = 112.
  4083	        let result = wb.read(ql_types::Address::new(0, 0, 1));
  4084	        assert_eq!(result, Value::Number(112.0));
  4085	    }

  4180	    /// MATCH exact + INDEX combination — the canonical replacement
  4181	    /// for VLOOKUP. Demonstrates both functions plus the
  4182	    /// scalar-argument result feeding into another formula.
  4183	    #[test]
  4184	    fn w5_54_index_match_pattern_e2e() {
  4185	        use ql_storage::NamedTarget;
  4186	        use std::sync::Arc;
  4187	        let mut wb = make_runtime_workbook();
  4188	        // Lookup keys in column A, values in column B.
  4189	        for (i, key) in ["alpha", "beta", "gamma", "delta"].iter().enumerate() {
  4190	            wb.put_at(0, i as u32, 0, Value::Text(Arc::from(*key)));
  4191	            wb.put_at(0, i as u32, 1, Value::Number(((i + 1) * 10) as f64));
  4192	        }
  4193	        wb.set_name(
  4194	            "Keys",
  4195	            NamedTarget::Range(ql_types::Range::new(0, 0, 0, 3, 0)),
  4196	        )
  4197	        .unwrap();
  4198	        wb.set_name(
  4199	            "Vals",
  4200	            NamedTarget::Range(ql_types::Range::new(0, 0, 1, 3, 1)),
  4201	        )
  4202	        .unwrap();
  4203	        let reg = default_registry();
  4204	        let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::new(&mut wb, &reg);
  4205	        // MATCH("gamma", Keys, 0) = 3 (1-based position of gamma).
  4206	        let m = rt
  4207	            .set_formula(0, 0, 2, "MATCH(\"gamma\", Keys, 0)")
  4208	            .unwrap();
  4209	        assert_eq!(m, Value::Number(3.0));
  4210	        // INDEX(Vals, MATCH("gamma", Keys, 0)) = 30.
  4211	        let v = rt
  4212	            .set_formula(0, 1, 2, "INDEX(Vals, MATCH(\"gamma\", Keys, 0))")
  4213	            .unwrap();
  4214	        assert_eq!(v, Value::Number(30.0));

  4280	    /// SUMIFS: two label columns + value column. Sum of values where
  4281	    /// labels1="a" AND labels2="y".
  4282	    #[test]
  4283	    fn w5_55_sumifs_two_conditions_e2e() {
  4284	        use ql_storage::NamedTarget;
  4285	        use std::sync::Arc;
  4286	        let mut wb = make_runtime_workbook();
  4287	        // Col A: labels1 [a, a, b, b]
  4288	        // Col B: labels2 [x, y, x, y]
  4289	        // Col C: vals    [1, 2, 3, 4]
  4290	        let l1 = ["a", "a", "b", "b"];
  4291	        let l2 = ["x", "y", "x", "y"];
  4292	        let vals = [1.0, 2.0, 3.0, 4.0];
  4293	        for (i, ((a, b), v)) in l1.iter().zip(l2.iter()).zip(vals.iter()).enumerate() {
  4294	            wb.put_at(0, i as u32, 0, Value::Text(Arc::from(*a)));
  4295	            wb.put_at(0, i as u32, 1, Value::Text(Arc::from(*b)));
  4296	            wb.put_at(0, i as u32, 2, Value::Number(*v));
  4297	        }
  4298	        wb.set_name(
  4299	            "LabelsA",
  4300	            NamedTarget::Range(ql_types::Range::new(0, 0, 0, 3, 0)),
  4301	        )
  4302	        .unwrap();
  4303	        wb.set_name(
  4304	            "LabelsB",
  4305	            NamedTarget::Range(ql_types::Range::new(0, 0, 1, 3, 1)),
  4306	        )
  4307	        .unwrap();
  4308	        wb.set_name(
  4309	            "Vals",
  4310	            NamedTarget::Range(ql_types::Range::new(0, 0, 2, 3, 2)),
  4311	        )
  4312	        .unwrap();
  4313	        let reg = default_registry();
  4314	        let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::new(&mut wb, &reg);
  4315	        let v = rt
  4316	            .set_formula(0, 0, 3, "SUMIFS(Vals, LabelsA, \"a\", LabelsB, \"y\")")
  4317	            .unwrap();
  4318	        // Only row 1 matches (a, y) → value 2.
  4319	        assert_eq!(v, Value::Number(2.0));
  4320	    }

  2581	    /// Phase 2B.7 audit closure (test gaps #5 and #6): the existing NAG
  2582	    /// tests don't exercise nested aggregates with named ranges. Ensure
  2583	    /// `SUM(AVERAGE(Sales))` (both aggregate; inner is the named-range arg)
  2584	    /// and `ROUND(SUM(Sales), 2)` (outer scalar, inner aggregate with the
  2585	    /// named range) both bind cleanly to the appropriate plan shapes.
  2586	    #[test]
  2587	    fn nag_05_nested_aggregates_with_named_range_bind_cleanly() {
  2588	        use ql_storage::NamedTarget;
  2589	        use ql_types::Range;
  2590	        let mut wb = make_runtime_workbook();
  2591	        wb.set_name("Sales", NamedTarget::Range(Range::new(0, 1, 0, 10, 0)))
  2592	            .unwrap();
  2593	        let reg = default_registry();
  2594	        let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::new(&mut wb, &reg);
  2595	
  2596	        // SUM(AVERAGE(Sales)) — outer SUM passes aggregate context to its
  2597	        // arg (the AVERAGE call), which in turn passes aggregate context
  2598	        // to its arg (the Sales NameRef). Both layers see aggregate
  2599	        // context; Sales binds to AggregateNameRef.
  2600	        // Phase 3.6 (W5-39): empty range. AVERAGE over empty = #DIV/0!
  2601	        // (per ql-functions::scalar_fns::average). Outer SUM propagates
  2602	        // the error.
  2603	        let v = rt.set_formula(0, 0, 0, "SUM(AVERAGE(Sales))").unwrap();
  2604	        assert_eq!(v, Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero));
  2605	    }

  2607	    #[test]
  2608	    fn nag_06_round_with_nested_sum_of_named_range_binds_cleanly() {
  2609	        use ql_storage::NamedTarget;
  2610	        use ql_types::Range;
  2611	        let mut wb = make_runtime_workbook();

  2615	        let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::new(&mut wb, &reg);
  2616	
  2617	        // ROUND is non-aggregate; its args bind in scalar context. The
  2618	        // FIRST arg here is SUM(Sales), which is itself a Function call —
  2619	        // recursive bind hits the SUM arm and switches to aggregate context
  2620	        // for ITS arg. Sales binds to AggregateNameRef. The outer ROUND
  2621	        // takes the SUM result + 2 in scalar context.
  2622	        // Phase 3.6 (W5-39): SUM(Sales) = 0 over empty range; ROUND(0, 2)
  2623	        // = 0.

  2625	        assert_eq!(v, Value::Number(0.0));

# Mega-audit verdict

The arc was not clean enough to call “Phase 4.3 functionally complete” without a W5-60 closure. The graph-storage fixes look directionally sound, and the registry count is real, but W5-53..W5-58 shipped multiple range-aware functions that now ignore or corrupt the very 2D shape contract introduced in W5-54. The worst class is silent wrong answers for explicit ranges with trailing blanks and for equal-length-but-different-shape ranges. W5-59 is useful as a handoff/protocol commit, but it oversells finality, leaves stale doc drift in place, and its “MANDATORY” audit protocol is social guidance, not enforceable process.

I could not save this report into a repo file because the active filesystem sandbox is read-only. This response is the full report.

## NEW HIGH

1. `WorkbookEnv::read_range_with_shape` drops trailing blank rows/cols, breaking explicit range semantics.

At [env.rs:130](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/env.rs:130), the W5-54 override says the shape reflects what was materialized. It clamps `end_row` / `end_col` to `Sheet::bounds()` at [env.rs:141](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/env.rs:141), and returns `(Vec::new(), 0, 0)` when the requested range is outside populated bounds at [env.rs:145](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/env.rs:145).

That is acceptable as a performance clamp for whole-column aggregate scans, but not for bounded explicit ranges used by shape-aware functions. `INDEX(A1:B10,10,2)` over a mostly blank range should return blank, not `#REF!`; current `INDEX` reads the clamped shape and bounds-checks against `rows` / `cols` at [range_fns.rs:444](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:444) and [range_fns.rs:508](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:508). Same issue affects `VLOOKUP` / `HLOOKUP` table shape at [range_fns.rs:579](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:579) and [range_fns.rs:635](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:635).

Fix before more function work: preserve requested bounded shape and fill out-of-bounds cells with blanks. Only clamp open-ended whole-row/whole-column ranges for tractability.

2. IFS and SUMPRODUCT claim shape rules but validate only flat length.

The comments say IFS-family ranges must have identical row x col dimensions at [range_fns.rs:731](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:731), but `parse_ifs_pairs` stores only `&[Value]` and `expected_len` at [range_fns.rs:797](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:797), then compares only `.len()` at [range_fns.rs:819](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:819). `SUMIFS` and `AVERAGEIFS` repeat the flat-length check at [range_fns.rs:846](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:846) and [range_fns.rs:915](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:915).

`SUMPRODUCT` has the same bug: it throws away `{ rows, cols }` at [range_fns.rs:967](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:967), then accepts any arrays with the same flat length at [range_fns.rs:980](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:980). Microsoft’s SUMPRODUCT docs state array arguments must have the same dimensions or return `#VALUE!`: https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/office/sumproduct-function-16753e75-9f68-4874-94ac-4d2145a2fd2e. The current implementation will accept `2x2` with `1x4` and silently multiply row-major.

Fix: carry `(values, rows, cols)` through IFS parsing and SUMPRODUCT validation. Add tests for `2x2` vs `1x4`, `1x3` vs `3x1`, and same length/different shape.

3. `SUMIF` / `AVERAGEIF` mismatched range behavior is oversimplified and silently wrong.

`SUMIF` documents zip/truncate behavior at [range_fns.rs:232](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:232), then implements flat `zip` at [range_fns.rs:268](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:268). `AVERAGEIF` mirrors it at [range_fns.rs:764](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:764). Excel’s SUMIF can use different-size ranges, but the semantics are top-left anchoring and resize, not zip/truncate. Microsoft’s SUMIF/SUMIFS error guidance explicitly notes SUMIF permits different-size ranges: https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/office/how-to-correct-a-value-error-in-the-sumif-sumifs-function-e65ce689-978e-44b1-a649-160ed5f13b5a.

This needs either correct anchored evaluation using range coordinates, or it must be downgraded in docs/matrix as a partial implementation with tests that pin the divergence.

## NEW MEDIUM

1. W5-59 handoff is stale about its own state.

The handoff says 24 commits unpushed and HEAD `d6a6bcdcfc5` plus finalization at [handoff:9](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/audits/2026-05-13-engine-session-final-handoff.md:9), but actual HEAD is `51fedf11c68` and the user brief says 25 commits unpushed. It also points to non-existent future audit artifacts `2026-05-13-phase-4.3-v2-session-audit-*` at [handoff:258](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/audits/2026-05-13-engine-session-final-handoff.md:258), while the actual files are `2026-05-13-session-final-megaudit-*`.

2. W5-59 doc-drift fixes were incomplete.

`MASTER-PLAN.md` header was updated, but body text still says the engine proceeds from HEAD `fc977815cd2` at [MASTER-PLAN.md:21](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:21) and still claims 816 workspace tests at [MASTER-PLAN.md:71](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:71). `known-gaps.md` still describes GAP-F-05 as `FnArg { Scalar(Value), Range(Vec<Value>) }` and says follow-up functions are “now safely unblocked” at [known-gaps.md:73](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/known-gaps.md:73), despite W5-54 changing the shape and W5-55/W5-58 shipping the follow-ups. The closed-gap summary repeats the stale tuple variant at [known-gaps.md:155](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/known-gaps.md:155).

3. `is_aggregate_function` is now a binder policy but the invariant is weak.

The comment is honest that the name is historical at [plan.rs:159](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:159), but the whitelist now mixes true scalar aggregates and range-aware scalar-return functions through [plan.rs:193](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:193). The test at [workbook_runtime.rs:2525](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:2525) manually mirrors the list; it verifies listed names are registered, but it will not catch a newly registered range-aware function missing from the binder whitelist unless the test author remembers to add it. Refactor to function metadata is overdue before Phase 4.7/4.10, or at minimum expose an authoritative `range_aware_names()` and assert all of them are accepted by binder tests.

4. Audit protocol is not actually enforceable.

`docs/process/audit-protocol.md` declares “MANDATORY” at [audit-protocol.md:3](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/process/audit-protocol.md:3), but nothing enforces it. The recovery section at [audit-protocol.md:304](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/process/audit-protocol.md:304) says what to do after findings, not what to do after violating the protocol. Missing: a session-end self-check, a commit-message checklist, a required `docs/audits/<scope>-protocol-check.md`, and CI/pre-commit scripts that can at least verify audit artifact presence for phase-close commits.

5. Test coverage is too happy-path for W5-53..W5-58.

The e2e tests for W5-55 use all 1D columns with matching shapes, e.g. [workbook_runtime.rs:4280](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:4280) and [workbook_runtime.rs:4342](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:4342). Missing obvious tests: explicit ranges with trailing blanks, `2D` vs `1D same flat length`, `SUMIF` different-size sum_range, lookup tables with blank trailing row/column, `SUMPRODUCT` dimension errors, and range-aware functions over a completely blank named range.

## NEW LOW

- `FunctionRegistry::names()` excludes range-aware names at [registry.rs:115](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/registry.rs:115). The protocol documents it, but it should be fixed or renamed before anyone uses it for introspection.
- Handoff registry wording is inconsistent: [handoff:45](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/audits/2026-05-13-engine-session-final-handoff.md:45) says “+5 → 78 unique + 2 aliases = 102 registry entries,” while [known-gaps.md:69](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/known-gaps.md:69) says “≈80 unique functions + 7 aliases.” Pick one counting scheme.
- The committed 5668-line planning Codex transcript is defensible provenance, but it is noisy. Future protocol should prefer preserving full raw tool output plus a short synthesis file, with the synthesis linked from the handoff.

## Per-commit assessment

W5-49: Honest architecture decision. The watch list was good, especially VEQ + supplemental edges and memory scale. It did not anticipate the range-aware dispatch blocker, but W5-53 later closed that.

W5-50: Graph revocation and supplemental scheduling look honestly shipped. Remaining VEQ + supplemental test gap is not newly critical, because `recompute_dirty` conservatively re-evaluates formulas with named ranges at [workbook_runtime.rs:987](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:987), but it should still be tested before Phase 4.7.

W5-51: Trig batch looks reasonably honest. I did not find a new blocking edge case beyond documented Inf/NaN strict-input behavior.

W5-52: Correctly fixed the clear-formula HIGH. The unamended W5-50 per-file test-count drift is harmless.

W5-53: Infrastructure direction was right, but it seeded the later shape problem by treating many functions as flat consumers and not requiring shape-aware tests.

W5-54: Lookup family exposed the core `read_range_with_shape` flaw. Shape exists in `FnArg`, but the environment can return a shrunken shape for explicit ranges.

W5-55: Not honestly complete for shape-sensitive Excel semantics. IFS and SUMPRODUCT need W5-60 fixes.

W5-56: Text wave is acceptable as partial Excel compatibility. Wildcards in SEARCH are documented deferred; UTF-16 divergence is documented.

W5-57: Math/hyperbolic batch appears honest, assuming the CEILING/FLOOR legacy semantics are intentionally pinned.

W5-58: Registry count closure is real, but stats inherit strict text-in-range divergence and trailing-blank shape issues. FN4-01 as “registry entries reached 102” is true; “functionally complete” is too strong.

W5-59: Useful but oversold. It created good handoff material, but left stale state and cannot make the protocol enforceable by assertion.

## W5-59 verdict

The handoff is directionally useful but not canonical. Its state header is stale, its future-audit artifact names do not match the actual session-final audit naming, and it still tells the next window to verify artifacts that were not landed under those names. The protocol is a good operating manual, but enforceability requires mechanical hooks or at least a required self-check artifact. The doc-drift fixes were partial: the exact header drift Codex flagged was fixed, but nearby body drift remains and GAP-F-05 still describes the pre-W5-54 tuple shape.

## What’s needed for W5-60 closure

1. Fix `read_range_with_shape` so bounded explicit ranges preserve requested shape and fill blanks; clamp only open-ended whole-row/whole-column ranges.
2. Make `SUMIFS` / `COUNTIFS` / `AVERAGEIFS` / `SUMPRODUCT` validate `(rows, cols)`, not only flat length.
3. Decide and implement correct `SUMIF` / `AVERAGEIF` different-size range semantics, or explicitly downgrade the matrix and docs.
4. Add regression tests for trailing blanks, blank-only ranges, same-length/different-shape ranges, and `SUMPRODUCT` dimension errors.
5. Repair W5-59 docs: HEAD/commit count, audit artifact names, `MASTER-PLAN.md` stale body lines, `known-gaps.md` stale `FnArg::Range(Vec<Value>)`.
6. Add a protocol self-check/recovery section so “MANDATORY” has an auditable trace.

# Mega-audit verdict

The arc was not clean enough to call “Phase 4.3 functionally complete” without a W5-60 closure. The graph-storage fixes look directionally sound, and the registry count is real, but W5-53..W5-58 shipped multiple range-aware functions that now ignore or corrupt the very 2D shape contract introduced in W5-54. The worst class is silent wrong answers for explicit ranges with trailing blanks and for equal-length-but-different-shape ranges. W5-59 is useful as a handoff/protocol commit, but it oversells finality, leaves stale doc drift in place, and its “MANDATORY” audit protocol is social guidance, not enforceable process.

I could not save this report into a repo file because the active filesystem sandbox is read-only. This response is the full report.

## NEW HIGH

1. `WorkbookEnv::read_range_with_shape` drops trailing blank rows/cols, breaking explicit range semantics.

At [env.rs:130](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/env.rs:130), the W5-54 override says the shape reflects what was materialized. It clamps `end_row` / `end_col` to `Sheet::bounds()` at [env.rs:141](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/env.rs:141), and returns `(Vec::new(), 0, 0)` when the requested range is outside populated bounds at [env.rs:145](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/env.rs:145).

That is acceptable as a performance clamp for whole-column aggregate scans, but not for bounded explicit ranges used by shape-aware functions. `INDEX(A1:B10,10,2)` over a mostly blank range should return blank, not `#REF!`; current `INDEX` reads the clamped shape and bounds-checks against `rows` / `cols` at [range_fns.rs:444](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:444) and [range_fns.rs:508](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:508). Same issue affects `VLOOKUP` / `HLOOKUP` table shape at [range_fns.rs:579](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:579) and [range_fns.rs:635](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:635).

Fix before more function work: preserve requested bounded shape and fill out-of-bounds cells with blanks. Only clamp open-ended whole-row/whole-column ranges for tractability.

2. IFS and SUMPRODUCT claim shape rules but validate only flat length.

The comments say IFS-family ranges must have identical row x col dimensions at [range_fns.rs:731](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:731), but `parse_ifs_pairs` stores only `&[Value]` and `expected_len` at [range_fns.rs:797](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:797), then compares only `.len()` at [range_fns.rs:819](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:819). `SUMIFS` and `AVERAGEIFS` repeat the flat-length check at [range_fns.rs:846](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:846) and [range_fns.rs:915](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:915).

`SUMPRODUCT` has the same bug: it throws away `{ rows, cols }` at [range_fns.rs:967](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:967), then accepts any arrays with the same flat length at [range_fns.rs:980](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:980). Microsoft’s SUMPRODUCT docs state array arguments must have the same dimensions or return `#VALUE!`: https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/office/sumproduct-function-16753e75-9f68-4874-94ac-4d2145a2fd2e. The current implementation will accept `2x2` with `1x4` and silently multiply row-major.

Fix: carry `(values, rows, cols)` through IFS parsing and SUMPRODUCT validation. Add tests for `2x2` vs `1x4`, `1x3` vs `3x1`, and same length/different shape.

3. `SUMIF` / `AVERAGEIF` mismatched range behavior is oversimplified and silently wrong.

`SUMIF` documents zip/truncate behavior at [range_fns.rs:232](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:232), then implements flat `zip` at [range_fns.rs:268](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:268). `AVERAGEIF` mirrors it at [range_fns.rs:764](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_fns.rs:764). Excel’s SUMIF can use different-size ranges, but the semantics are top-left anchoring and resize, not zip/truncate. Microsoft’s SUMIF/SUMIFS error guidance explicitly notes SUMIF permits different-size ranges: https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/office/how-to-correct-a-value-error-in-the-sumif-sumifs-function-e65ce689-978e-44b1-a649-160ed5f13b5a.

This needs either correct anchored evaluation using range coordinates, or it must be downgraded in docs/matrix as a partial implementation with tests that pin the divergence.
